# Ideas and what the Arena and Prosperity data said

Every strategy idea we have tested, on the Arena's own simulator first (7 Nov on), then on Prosperity data, with its result, and the ideas still waiting for a test.

<!-- ideas-index:start -->

<!-- ideas-index:end -->

**A result here is a prior about the Arena, not a verdict.** The Arena's products, bots, matching and scoring will differ from Prosperity's. So an idea that loses here stays on this list, marked `dropped here`, and is tested again on the Arena's data. An idea that wins here is tested again too. `ON_THE_DAY.md` ("Every round") points here.

**Keeping it up to date:**
- When a test runs, add the result to its row: the date, the data, the command and the numbers. Keep the earlier results and add the new one under them.
- After any edit to a row, `uv run python -m experiments.ideas_index` rewrites the index at the top (`python/tests/test_ideas_index.py` fails until it is current).
- Each verdict is one of these:
  - `kept`: it is in the template;
  - `dropped here`: it is not in the template, and is re-tested on the day anyway;
  - `measured`: a fact about the data, not a strategy;
  - `open`: tested, with no conclusion yet.
- **`--resting` results before 2026-10-13's review fixes are a little high.** Until then a resting order crossed by the next snapshot could also fill from that tick's market trades at the same price, which used the same orders twice. The fix took 0.3–3.6% off the template alone (P3 round 1: 68,043 to 67,446) and 13–16% off its runs against copies of itself. The rows below that were re-run say so; older `--resting` numbers elsewhere were not re-run.
- On the day, for each round, go down the "Re-test on the Arena" column and run the rows whose condition fits that round's products, again in every round: the rounds get harder, so an Arena result from an earlier round is a prior too.

**What a market we didn't write said about these priors** (2026-10-27; ABIDES's RMSC04, 10 days, "An independent generator: ABIDES's RMSC04" and the rows after it; LOBSTER's real book is still to come, `docs/MANUAL_TODO.md`):
- **Held:** the statistics and the tools. The grid's pick rule, the held-out checks and StepM named the settings that also won on held-out ABIDES days; `diagnose`'s notes read the market correctly (large-tick, trades move the wall mid, a thin margin); the fill-model spread (`all` against `worse` against resting) mattered as much as there.
- **Did not hold:** the playbook. Prosperity's one wall at the value, its uninformed flow and its free inventory are what make plain market making pay. In an agent market with informed and momentum traders, no fair mode finds the value (all 34 ticks off), passive fills lose to adverse selection, moves continue, and every quoting setting of the template as committed loses with resting orders (doing nothing beats it). The shuffle test tells the two apart in one command: shuffling Prosperity's ticks changes nothing, shuffling ABIDES's turns a loss into millions.
- **What paid there instead:** a fair that leans on the flow and the trend (`lin_rho3` 3, `lin_momentum` 0.5 over 100 ticks: +1.1M to +1.2M under every replay mode, and +1.27M inside the market itself, where the template as committed loses 230k; re-run 2026-10-28, the fourth audit's R13 and R14), or pulling the side a move runs over (`gate_move` 2 over 50 ticks: +0.7M); both cost a little on Prosperity, so both stay off by default. Smoothing, per-side edges, a take reserve, the hysteretic mid and the join gate did not help.
- **So on the day:** run the shuffle test and `quote_markouts` on the first download before trusting any row below. If the Arena's market looks like Prosperity's, these rows stand as priors; if it looks like ABIDES's, start from the linear fair and the gate, and from a bot that does nothing as the benchmark to beat.

## On the Arena (7 Nov on)

Measured on the organisers' own simulator (the root `simulator/`, or its Rust port `rust/arena_sim`) on their tapes: the playground's
`data/sample.csv.gz` and ABIDES hours written in its format. The five families in `python/bots/qminers/families/`, the
template `ArenaBot` and their grids. These are the Arena's engine and data, not a stand-in; re-measure each on the round's tape.

| Idea | Result on the Arena's data | Verdict here | Re-test on the Arena |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Calibration draws need provenance, not just matching filenames** (`gen_calibrate`) | 2026-11-08, repository checks and actual two-seed 40-s CLI smoke with empirical ladders and one latent path, repeated from cache. `uv run pytest python/tests/test_gen_cache.py python/tests/test_gen_ladders.py python/tests/test_gen_inventory.py python/tests/test_gen_pnl_probe.py python/tests/test_docs.py -q`: **56 passed**, including nine cache tests. Cached draws now require matching parameter/seed/duration/source/runtime identity and output SHA256; legacy files and changed bytes fail. Generation is staged, latent regeneration must match the existing tape before publication, and mixed-directory writes cannot replace the README/params before rejection. Reproduction smoke command in `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md`, "Calibration cache provenance". Existing generator tape hashes remain unchanged. | `fixed`: prevents silently comparing old draws under new parameter labels; legacy tapes remain usable via explicit score/stats commands | Keep distinct directories for changed models and carry the provenance sidecars with shared generated tapes |

Within the limit (BURST 100, no penalty) the best is -28,535 (TRIGGER 1500, HOLD 1000): the move after a big sweep (~0.9 ticks within 1 s for 800+ lots) doesn't pay the spread crossed twice (~1 tick). Over the limit the penalty comes on top: best -534,648 (1500/400/50 ms, penalty 283,695), worst -46.2M (penalty 43.5M). With the flat penalty (~10 a millisecond over) any over-limit plan needs a move larger than the spread within milliseconds; round 1 has none. <br>- **The maker with more room** (2026-11-07 15:55, R1-F8 with `LIMIT` raised, `uv run python -m arena.qgrid python/bots/qminers/candidates/r1_F8_take_join.py data/arena/round1/round1.csv --grid LIMIT=100,110,125,150,200`): pnl before the penalty rises 519k / 553k / 597k / 645k / 667k, but with no way back the position sits over 100 for 1,900-3,500 s (penalty 19.0M-34.9M). With an IOC back to `UNWIND_TO` the moment it passes 100 (`--set UNWIND=100 --grid LIMIT=110,125,150 --grid UNWIND_TO=60,90,100`): best 239,319 (110/100, penalty 290,417); every cell under F8, since each breach costs at least 50 (5 ms over) plus the crossed spread, and breaches come in the thousands. An IOC unwind inside the limit (`--grid UNWIND=60,80,95 --grid UNWIND_TO=0,30,50`) pays the spread and +18k to +45k of penalty (the IOC is not counted against the other side's room): +130,738 at best, -123,661 at worst <br>- **The win: quote all the room the limit gives.** The same LIMIT 100 cell with `SIZE_TOUCH=150` +219,165 / +52,108: the reducing side quotes up to LIMIT + \|position\|, not a fixed 100. Then `--grid SIZE_TOUCH=100,150,200 --grid TOPUP=0,10,30 --grid SKEW=80,100` (`TOPUP`: add an order behind ours at the touch once that much room is free, so the side's exposure is always the full room): **`SIZE_TOUCH=200, TOPUP=10, SKEW=100` +263,282 / +61,717**, 7,764 fills, 521,952 lots, \|inv\| q99.7 100, no penalty; `SIZE_TOUCH=150` +252,984 / +58,738; `TOPUP=0` +221,596. `--grid TOPUP=1,5,10 --grid KEEP_DEPTH=1,2 --grid WIND_DOWN_S=0,60`: `TOPUP` flat (+263.6k), `WIND_DOWN_S=0` +322. `CANCEL_ALONE=0` (keep an order left in front of the touch) +242,542; `LIMIT=96` +257,246 <br>- **`run_review` (`uv run python -m experiments.day.run_review DIR` on an `exchange-simulator` full-logs run):** F2 9,932 maker fills, edge +0.48 ticks a lot, the mid our way after +0.06 / +0.06 / +0.08 at 1 / 5 / 30 s; F3 12,976 maker fills, edge +0.47, +0.01 / +0.02 / +0.03: no adverse selection, so the PnL is the spread times the volume and the volume is set by the limit. | **A generative model of round 1's tape: choose on many draws of its generator, not on the one file** (`python/experiments/day/tape_gen.py` fits and draws, `tape_eval.py` runs the candidates on the draws; the premise: the private tape is another draw of the same generator, same length) | 2026-11-07, round1.csv (12,000 s). `uv run python -m experiments.day.tape_gen --fit data/arena/round1/round1.csv --params-out data/arena/round1/tape_gen/params_r1.json` (5 s), then `uv run python -m experiments.day.tape_eval --params data/arena/round1/tape_gen/params_r1.json --real data/arena/round1/round1.csv --seeds 1-30 --candidates F3,F5,F6,F7,F1` (and `--seeds 1-5 --candidates M2 --profile`). <br>- **What the fit found**: limit inserts one Poisson stream, 180/s at a 1- and a 2-tick spread alike, sides 50/50, never crossing; every G order has a cancel row (all but 66 of 2.16M, filled or not), lifetimes the same by offset and size (median 0.34 s); IOC orders Poisson (8.0/s, gaps CV 1.00), the side independent of the last (P(same) 0.501) and of the past 2 s move, sizes independent of the touch, priced at the touch (52%) or one through (46%). **A latent price**: at a 1-tick spread a bid lands at the touch 80% of the time when the mid is up 0.5 s later and 23% when down (asks mirrored), while the PAST move changes nothing; the mid 5 s after an IOC order rises with its size, +0.10 ticks (10 lots or fewer) to +0.90 (1,000+). <br>- **The model**: a real-valued latent mid moved only by the IOC orders (each by its size bin's mean 5 s impact), bids at floor(latent) - k and asks at floor(latent) + 1 + k (k from the 1-tick offsets: 0 55%, 1 39%, 2 6%), clamped off the other touch; on a latent move the orders left on the wrong side cancel within Exp(0.2 s) (`stale_s`, calibrated on the mid's changes a second and its move after a 1-tick move). <br>- **Variants dropped** (3 seeds each, F3/F5/F6/F7 against real 426/456/483/497k): pure zero-intelligence offsets from the touch: no momentum (+0.01 at 5 s against +0.22), mid 0.81 changes/s (2.57); a latent mid in whole-tick jumps with no stale cancels: 584/733/510/498k (5 seeds), the guard's F5 far first; plus informed IOC orders (5% / 15% / 60% sent the latent's way): 535/704/478/472k, 406/598/382/381k, all negative at 60%; stale cancels 50 / 100 ms with the continuous latent: 263/292/478/474k, 374/400/503/497k; the touch's own offset table at a 2+ tick spread (`wide_table`): 2-tick share 9.5%, 712/716/656/662k. <br>- **Validation of the kept model** (profile of seed 1 against the real tape): 1-tick spread 97.4% (93.0%), 2 ticks 2.5% (6.9%); touch 933 lots (1,065, -12%), within 5 ticks 2,311 (2,545); 7.89 IOC orders/s (7.89), size median 57 / p90 327 (60 / 345); 2.56 mid changes/s (2.57); the mid after a trade +0.19 at 1 s, +0.20 at 5 s (+0.22 / +0.22), after one over the median +0.27 / +0.29 (+0.32 / +0.32), after a 1-tick move +0.05 at 5 s (+0.06). **PnL, real against the generated mean over 30 seeds (sd, 5th percentile, ratio)**: F3 425,553 / 480,799 (8,746, 465,391, 1.13); Pairwise on the seeds: F5 > F3 30/30 (+28.7k, real +30.3k), F6 > F5 30/30 (+18.3k, real +27.3k), **but F7 < F6 30/30 (-4.3k, se 0.5k; real +13.5k)**. <br>- **Robust grid on F7 over 10 draws** (`uv run python -m experiments.day.tape_eval --params data/arena/round1/tape_gen/params_r1.json --real data/arena/round1/round1.csv --seeds 1-10 --base F7 --grid SIZE_TOUCH=50,65,80 --grid SIZE_DEEP=60,80,110 --grid SIZE_NARROW=100,115,150 --grid DYING_DEEP=0,80 --grid MOM_JOIN=0,1`, 108 cells, chosen by the generated mean only): first SIZE_DEEP 60 with MOM_JOIN 0, 528,421 (+4,370 over F7, se 612, 10/10 seeds; 22 cells beat F7 by 2+ se, every top-15 one with MOM_JOIN 0); **on the real tape once: 486,943, -9,621 against F7**: no R1-G1 made. | **The tape generator's structure: a latent price moved by √-impact, a calm and a volatile regime, exogenous jumps announced by widening placement** (`python/experiments/day/gen_identify.py`; Phase 1 of `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md`, whose "Phase 1 findings" has the tables) | 2026-11-08 01:30, round1.csv, round 1's private rebuild, round2_public.csv and its mirror, round3_public.csv. `uv run python -m experiments.day.gen_identify data/arena/round3/round3_public.csv --cache R3.npz --json R3.json` per tape (about 10 s once cached), then `--compare R1.json R1P.json R2.json R3.json`. <br>- **Impact**: every aggressive order moves the latent (the mean of the new bid and ask inserts' prices) by c·√(traded lots), permanent from 0.1 s to 300 s, with no random part on r1 (the jump's variance equals the estimator's noise). c 0.024 on r1; in the calm / volatile state 0.028 / 0.046 on r2 and 0.033 / 0.058 on r3. <br>- **Exogenous moves**: none on r1 (0.06 ticks² at 1 s, flat to 300 s). On r2 / r3: 14 / 13.6 ticks² a second, against 1.1 / 4.4 from the impacts; 97 / 96% of the 1-s residual's variance is in steps of 2+ ticks (kurtosis 427 / 423). <br>- **Jumps** (3+ ticks in 0.2 s): r2 313 (52 a 1,000 s), r3 389 (65); \|size\| median 5.6 / 5.2, q99 63 / 64; up share 0.45 / 0.51; permanent; gaps' CV 2.8 / 2.6; sign independent of the previous one's (0.48). <br>- **Regimes** (a Gaussian mixture on log 60-s variance, BIC): r1 one state; r2 / r3 calm 47 / 32% and volatile 53 / 68% of blocks, runs of 2-3.5 minutes. The volatile state brings F orders 10/s against 8, insert width 2.8-3.8 against 2.1-2.4, the 1-tick share 75% against 93%. <br>- **Placement**: offsets from the latent: r1 mean 1.03, sd 0.56; r3 volatile 1.95 / 1.71. Sizes independent of the offset. <br>- **Cancels**: every order has a cancel row, median lifetime 0.31-0.35 s; the stale hazard is 1.5-2.4x the fresh one on every tape. <br>- **Aggressive flow**: side a coin flip, uninformed (corr with the next exogenous move 0.007 or less on r2 / r3); sizes the same on every tape (median 60, p99 about 1,540, Hill α 1.7); Poisson on r1, clustered on r2 / r3 (10-s dispersion 5.5-5.9); limit priced from the latent (1.0-1.3 ticks beyond it), not from the touch. <br>- **Precursor**: for the first jump after 30 s without one, the spread goes from 1.0-1.3 to 2.2-2.7 and the insert width from 2.1-2.9 to 4.0-4.7, about 2 s before the onset. P(jump within 5 s \| spread 3+) 0.83 / 0.77 (lift 20 / 11). Insert-width episodes over q90 end in a jump within 5 s 90 / 71% of the time, lead median 1.6 / 2.75 s. <br>- **Direction**: nothing before the onset predicts it (\|corr\| ≤ 0.13). After it: a trade at a 3+ spread has the next move's side 64-66% of the time (73-76% for 5+ ticks). 5 ms after the first one, stale lots beyond the new latent are worth 1.21M / 344k ticks·lots (38k / 17k capped at 100 lots). **The race** (jumps of 5+ ticks): the first trade the jump's way comes a median 0.065 s after the reveal (the first order priced at the new level). 5 ms after that trade the stale side still holds 1,319 / 1,775 lots worth 1.54M / 2.61M ticks·lots (98k / 132k capped at 100 lots); 100 ms after, 0.65M / 1.44M. So the book drains slowly. The trigger's false positives are not costed. Aggressive counts are Poisson within the calm state (10-s dispersion 1.0-1.5) and burst within the volatile one (6.3-7.7) | `measured`: one generator, two regimes. Round 1 is the calm state throughout; rounds 2 and 3 are 53 / 68% volatile, with jumps (exogenous, permanent, clustered, random in sign) that the placement's widening announces about 2 s ahead. What moves between tapes is chiefly the volatile share, then c and the jump rate | Each new tape: run `gen_identify` and `--compare` first (the volatile share, c, jumps a 1,000 s, the precursor's hit rate). Then Phase 2 (tape_gen v2 with the regimes, jumps and pre-jump widening) and a bot rule: pull quotes when the spread or insert width crosses its q90-q97, and take the stale side once the first trade at a wide spread shows the direction |

Spread 1 / 2 / 3+ at the second's start: 288k / 110k / 29k (59 / 135 / 108 a second). The best 1% of seconds make 25%, the worst 1% cost 18%. <br>- **Predictability** (50-ms samples, correlation with the mid's move 0.1 / 0.4 / 1 / 5 / 30 / 120 s ahead, first 75%): the filter's gap (impact x3) 0.24 / 0.31 / 0.27 / 0.11 / 0.04 / 0.03; the touch imbalance 0.10 / 0.09 / 0.07 / 0.02 / 0.01 / 0; signed F √-impact over 1 s 0.12 / 0.16 / 0.14 / 0.03 / -0.02 / 0; the 60-s vol proxy, time since the last jump and a backward insert-centre gap about 0. The mid's distance from its 1,200-s EMA against the next 600 s: corr -0.41 on r3 (7 non-overlapping windows), -0.24 on r2, -0.21 on r1; against the next 300 s -0.22 / -0.06 / -0.25. Weak, inconsistent, and 7-35 independent windows a tape: `dropped here`. <br>- **Maker economics** (a one-lot quote at the touch the F order hits; markout in ticks a lot, fill against the mid h s later). Front of the queue on r3: calm +0.17 / +0.19 / +0.32 at 1 / 5 / 30 s, volatile -0.38 / -0.34 / -0.02. Volatile far from a jump: +0.12 / +0.15 / +0.42. Near a jump: -0.70 / -0.67 / -0.31. At the back of the queue (the F order takes the whole level): -1.21 at 1 s overall, -0.34 in calm. By the F order's size: up to 30 lots +0.18 at 1 s, over 500 lots -1.76. Level 2 (a tick behind) in calm: +0.40 / +0.50. <br>- **The filter's gap decides a maker's fill** (at a 1-tick spread, the gap at the fill the maker's way, 1 / 5 s): r3 +0.475 / +0.435 a lot when it favours the maker (19,722 fills), -0.83 / -0.76 when against (21,766); r2 +0.44 / +0.44 against -0.24 / -0.25; r1 +0.47 / +0.47 against -0.02 / -0.02. At 0.5+ ticks the maker's way: +0.69 / +0.61 / +0.69 (r3 / r2 / r1). A 50-lot quote at the front, on the favoured side only, is worth +46 / +44 / +57 ticks a second (front-of-queue fills, before the inventory cap). A jump flag (no quote within 10 s of a 1-s mid move of 3+) gives only +0.14 a lot on r3 and removes 75% of its fills. <br>- **Other:** 67% of the 60-s blocks are volatile, in runs of 1-16 blocks; 385 of 389 jumps fall in volatile blocks; the last 120 s are ordinary; the F sizes are continuous (1,831 distinct values). | **A filter of the generator's hidden fair price as three extra features of R2-L9's ridge (R3-F1): new market orders land around a latent price the mid only follows, so a Bayesian filter of that price from where they land should beat the hand-made features** (`python/experiments/day/latent_filter.py`, offline; `python/bots/qminers/candidates/r3_F1_latent_filter.py`, `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md` Phase 3.2) | 2026-11-08 00:57-01:25. The filter: a hidden-Markov filter over the latent reference bid lb (17 integer states around the bid). Bids land at lb - k and asks at lb + 1 + k with k 0 / 1 / 2 at 55 / 39 / 6% (round 1's offsets), clamped one tick off the other touch, with a likelihood floor of 0.01. lb random-walks at 2 steps/s each way, and a market taker's filled lots move it by 3x tape_gen's impact table (round 1's 5-s mid move by size). The signal is gap = E[lb] + 0.5 - mid. <br>- **Offline** (`uv run python -m experiments.day.latent_filter TAPE --set impact_scale=3`, 50-ms samples): the correlation of gap with the mid's move 0.1 / 0.4 / 1 / 5 s ahead, first 75% \| last 25%, with the touch imbalance's at 0.4 s in brackets: round1 (impact x1) 0.36 / 0.51 / 0.40 / 0.18 \| 0.35 / 0.50 / 0.39 / 0.18 (0.36 \| 0.36), the rebuilt private tape the same; round2_public 0.29 / 0.26 / 0.18 / 0.10 \| 0.11 / 0.15 / 0.09 / 0.05 (0.06 \| 0.05), its 5-s slope 0.98 (unbiased in ticks); hi_vol_fast1.25 (round 2's stitched tape) 0.23 / 0.20 / 0.13 / 0.06 \| 0.16 / 0.16 / 0.10 / 0.05 (0.03 \| 0.03); round3_public 0.24 / 0.31 / 0.27 / 0.11 \| 0.33 / 0.41 / 0.35 / 0.15 (0.09 \| 0.12). The impact scale's best is x1 on round 1, x3-5 on round 2 and x3 on round 3. Phase 1's sqrt rule (0.0365 sqrt(lots), filled lots or the order's size) is between x1 and x3 on round 3. Rate 0.5-8/s, eps 0.003-0.03, W 8-12 and the k weights change the correlations by under 0.01; W 4 and eps 0.1 lose. <br>- **Big moves:** on round2_public, when |gap| >= 2 ticks (3.1% of the time) the next 1 s goes the gap's way 74% of the time, mean +2.8 ticks; 5+ ticks right 28%, wrong 8%. Over 5 s: 62%, +3.4, 37% / 23%. Of all 5+ tick 1-s moves the filter called 27% at their start (|gap| >= 1 the right way) and was against 13%. On round3_public, |gap| >= 2 (10% of the time): 78%, +3.3 ticks, 37% / 5%. Round 2's jumps are 500-1,000+ lot sweeps through 5-9 levels, after inserts widen to 3-5 ticks behind both touches. <br>- **Against the F limits** (Phase 1: an F's limit is the latent mid +-1) on round 3: corr 0.32, slope 0.58; the implied latent's sd about the mid is 2.9 ticks and the filter's error sd 2.8. The filter sees only part of how far the latent strays. <br>- **The bot:** R2-L9 plus gap, gap\*\|gap\|/3 and the posterior sd (FEATURE_COUNT 39; FILTER = 0 is L9 to the unit, 317,057 on round2_public). `uv run exchange-simulator --strategy FILE --backtest TAPE --log-dir logs/x --no-logs`, F1 / L9: **round3_public 480,469 / 424,730 (+55.7k, +13%; 500-s paired segments 7/11 won, t +1.57; first 75% +46.2k, last 25% +9.5k)**; round2_public 303,720 / 317,057 (-4.2%, t -0.78, 4/11); round 2's hi_vol_fast1.25 1,012,787 / 1,020,848 (-0.8%); round1 152,188 / 134,626 (+13%). Breach 0, \|pos\| q95 100 on every run. It trades more (r3 572k lots vs 460k, r2 527k vs 303k). Run time is +10-16% over L9 (r3 205 s vs 187 s locally). <br>- **TAKE_EDGE 0.3** on r3 public: F1 495,178 (+14.7k over F1 at 0.15), L9 429,430 (+4.7k), so F1 at 0.3 is +70.4k over L9 (8/11 segments, t +2.38). <br>- **In Rust** (`ridge_maker --set FILTER=1`, 01:20-01:40; R3-F1 on round2_public gives pnl.csv and result.json byte for byte as the Python file, 303720.00, 3,795 fills; FILTER 0 unchanged). Every base gains on every tape (+11% to +29%), both halves each time, and max drawdown falls to ~15k everywhere. Quotes: 47,795 one-lot fills, +0.41 a lot at the fill, +0.14 / +0.35 / +0.55 at 1 / 5 / 30 s (+6.6k / +16.7k / +26.3k; F3 - F1 +24.7k); 199 of them while QS_RATE's switch was off (resting from before; -5.6 a lot at 5 s, -1.1k), and pulling those (QS_PULL 1) 495,475 (-9.7k); 3,872 with the filter's gap 0.5+ against the quote (-0.62 a lot at 1 s, +0.47 at 5 s), and withdrawing that side (QUOTE_GAP 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 1.0) 501,145 / 497,968 / 497,848 / 500,263. Takes: 12,263 fills, 563,769 lots, -0.50 a lot at the fill, +0.77 / +0.90 at 1 / 5 s; 8,051 lots fewer than F1's (-1.4%): the one-lot quotes' reserve, at most a lot a take, and with POSITION_LIMIT = EXPOSURE = 100 the limit's bound (limit - position - live same-side lots) binds whatever FT_ROOM_LIVE says; fills arrive synchronously and an IOC in flight is reserved, so no take is sized on a stale position. On validation the quotes made about 111k extra fills for +20.8k (0.19 a fill) against 48.7k for +24.7k locally (0.51 a fill); the results folders hold only pnl.csv, so the cause is not visible | `measured`: the record reproduces and no experiment ran on a stale branch; F3's order handling has no bug worth fixing (late cancels and stale quotes about -1k each, every fix tried loses 4-95k). One conclusion leans the other way on F3's own base: **FT_EDGE 0.25 beats 0.15 on r3 public and on three r3b draws (+41k over 42,000 s, both halves)**, though only t +0.6 on r3 public and t +1.5 on seed 202 alone; plausibly +5-15k on a 12,000-s validation tape (a one-constant file: F3 with TAKE_EDGE = 0.25). QS_RATE 3.5 (+3.2k public) loses 70k on the draws; the in-limit jump take and the quiet-trade take are ties | Round 4: on the new base, grid FT_EDGE 0.15-0.35 and QS_RATE 2-5 on the public tape and fresh generator draws before the first upload; run quote_audit on a full-logs run of the base |

The template keeps 45–51% of it against one rival and 20% against all three. Round 1: alone 53.9k; against DU 25.4k, Baguette Fromage 28.6k, DTU 29.5k; against all three 12.6k. <br>- **Pepper Root's drift pays everyone:** the template makes 232.6k alone and 228.4k against all three. <br>- **Totals against all three:** the template comes last, 241.0k against 253.3–255.4k (round 1) and 238.8k against 254.9–256.9k (round 2). <br>**`--resting`:** the template loses on Osmium. Round 1: −46k against DU, −374k against Baguette Fromage, −377k against all three, where DU makes +221k. Round 2: −43k, −434k and −445k. <br>- **The cause** *(checked on round 1, day 0, against Baguette Fromage)*: a rival's resting order far from fair can be larger than the market maker's wall, and `_wall` picks it. At tick 2,400 a 40-lot bid at 9,906 beat the 23-lot wall at 9,991, and the template's fair became 9,957 against a recorded 10,001.5. The template then sold into the real bids. The fair it saw was 5+ off on 36% of ticks. <br>- **Default mode is immune:** rivals' orders are never visible there. <br>- **Baguette Fromage is hit too:** it loses 65–80k on Osmium against DU and DTU in the same setting (cause not analysed) <br>**2026-10-23, after the per-tick priority fix** (`e003e81`: per tick the bots' leftover orders now meet the market trades best price first, where before they met them in arrival order; before/after on the same code, `--engine rust`), default mode, P4 round 1 (`uv run --with jsonpickle python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 1 --data p4 --vs ...`, Python engine), the template's total before / after: against all three 240.8k / 247.7k (Osmium 15.5k / 15.6k, Pepper Root 225.3k / 232.1k; the rivals make 250.3–252.8k, so it is still last), against DU 254.1k / 258.7k, Baguette Fromage 259.9k / 264.4k, DTU 268.0k / 268.2k. The gain is Pepper Root's: its bids, nearer fair than the rivals', now win the trades they price better. `--resting` is unchanged <br>2026-11-04, re-run for the guidebook (`data/osmium_rivals.csv`, `data/rival_fair.csv`): orders living one tick, Osmium alone 55,902 (was 53.9k), against DU 27,658, Baguette Fromage 30,106, DTU 32,492 (25.4k, 28.6k, 29.5k), against all three 15,632 (the 23 Oct 15.6k). With `--resting --requote`, the −371k / +34.2k reproduce (−371,138 / +34,151) only with `--set PARAMS.ASH_COATED_OSMIUM.smoothing=0 --set PARAMS.ASH_COATED_OSMIUM.stop_loss=None`; the committed template (smoothing 0.9, stop loss 5,500) gives **−3,168** with `wall_mid` and **+70,348** with `sized_mid`. Osmium itself still uses `wall_mid`. | `measured`; under `--resting`, a flaw in `wall_mid`, fixed by `fair: sized_mid` (the wall-mid definitions row) | If the market is shared: run `--vs` with every rival bot available, in the engine mode the playground matches. Check that the fair value ignores large orders far from the touch (`ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Is `wall_mid` still the market maker's price?") |
| **`skew` against Kelp's long position** | Kelp sits at +50 about 10% of the time, and `skew` didn't fix it; no numbers recorded (`docs/TODO_DONE.md`, "Explain Kelp's long drift") <br>2026-10-26, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.kelp_drift` (P3 round 1, three days, the template as committed): <br>- **The position is the takers' flow, not a bias.** On each day mirrored (prices reflected, bids for asks, buyers for sellers), Kelp's mean position is exactly the negative of the day as is (+21.5 to +35.2 becomes −21.5 to −35.2) and the PnL is the same to the unit, under `all`, `worse` and `--resting`; Resin's small positions flip the same way. So the template's fair and quotes are symmetric, and the long comes from the flow: 34–36% of Kelp's trades at the touch are buys (41–43% of the volume), against 49–50% on Resin. Under `all` our passive buys exceed our passive sells 1.24–1.32 to 1 (3,930–4,060 lots against 3,041–3,247 a day), and takes sell most of it back (1,760–1,821 against 829–1,010). <br>- **The flow isn't informed** (Harris's test): our position's correlation with the mark's change over the next 10–1,000 ticks is −0.10 to +0.18 over the days and modes, mostly positive; Resin +0.02 to +0.06. <br>- **`skew` costs PnL at every step**, three days, skew 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 ticks at the full limit: `all` 18,698 / 18,210 / 17,350 / 15,893 (mean position +24.5 / +19.3 / +13.9 / +9.7); `worse` 14,530 / 12,370 / 10,498 / 9,514 (+26.4 / +14.1 / +7.0 / +5.1); `--resting` 16,834 / 13,208 / 10,298 / 8,096 (+31.3 / +14.9 / +6.9 / +4.7). Holding the position is what earning on one-sided, uninformed flow costs, and here it costs nothing measurable | `dropped here` (default 0.0) | Sweep `skew` per product, using the coefficient the literature gives (`references/wiki/concepts/optimal-quoting-models.md`). A product held on one side where `arena.diagnose`'s buy share is far from half is the flow, not a bias: skew it only if Harris's test on the `--out` timeline is clearly negative |

Pepper Root rises about 1,000 a day in both rounds, yet loses in round 2: unexplained | `kept` for Osmium; `dropped here` for Pepper Root: the published traders hold a long position and earn about 80k a day from the drift (next row) | For a drifting product, compare PnL across rounds and days before trusting one; never tune on one round |
| **Inventory control through quote size** (Bouchaud et al. eq. 17.4: each quote times `max(1 ± κ·gap, 0)`, so the side that adds to the gap from `target` shrinks; Harris's "change the sizes") | 2026-10-13, the template's new `size_skew` (κ, with the gap as a share of the limit), gridded with `--modes all,worse`, e.g. `uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 1 --grid PARAMS.KELP.size_skew=0,0.5,1,2,4 --grid PARAMS.KELP.skew=0,2,5 --modes all,worse`: <br>- **P3 round 1, Kelp:** 0 is best by PnL, worst day and `worse` fills (81,292). 0.5 costs 189 and wins the neighbourhood measure by 177. 4 costs 4,084. Near the target, its full-room ask (about 150 lots) dwarfs fills of at most 8 lots; far below the target, `skew` has already moved the ask out of reach (positions and fills on round 1 day 0). <br>- **Price skew costs more everywhere:** Kelp `skew` 2 costs 3.9k, Osmium `skew` 5 costs 12.2k. <br>This fits the diagnosis that Prosperity's flow is uninformed and mean-reverting, so the slow margin beats the fast one (`arena.diagnose`) <br>2026-11-04, re-run for the guidebook (`uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 1 --data p3 --grid PARAMS.KELP.size_skew=0,0.5,1,2,4 --modes all,worse --engine rust`; `data/size_controls.csv`): on today's 72,674 baseline, `size_skew` 0.5 costs 91 and 4 costs 2,716 (was 189 and 4,084 on 81,292); `make_size` 20 costs 3 credits on Kelp. | `dropped here` (default 0.0; the parameter stays in the template) | If `arena.diagnose` shows informed, one-sided or persistent flow, or the score penalises risk: grid `size_skew` against `skew` per product |

Edge 1 is the best reply to every rival edge (a tie with 2 against 2), so neither side gains by moving off 1 vs 1. Alone it costs −2.5k under `all`, +2.7k under `worse`, −0.5k with `--resting` (`uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 1 --match-trades worse --set PARAMS.RAINFOREST_RESIN.make_edge=1`, and `all`; the decision is in `docs/TODO_DONE.md`). <br>- **Found on the way: two copies of Pepper Root's drift bet fight over it.** Per tick, one copy holds +80 all day and the other's `stop_loss` (4.5k) fires within 60 ticks, every day (worst day −5.3k; which one wins is the arrival shuffle). At its target the template offers its whole position and 80 more (`make_size` None) one tick above fair, and a rival below the same target bids up to that price with its skew, so the full room changes hands in one tick. Quoting at `make_edge` 0 ourselves wins back at most 1.1k (Resin, against the undercutter). Pepper Root with `make_size` 10 against a copy: −32.9k / −21.0k under `all` / `worse`. A generator's design, like the round's fixed sum. <br>- **Small:** 0.07–0.14 follower ticks per CIRCLE tick. CIRCLE's last k moves predict the follower's next k ticks with an sd of 4.3 (OVAL) to 12.7 ticks (SQUARE), against half-spreads of 4–6.5 and the followers' own k-tick sds of 90–210. The follow test can't see it: at one exact lag the lead is a small part of B's whole move over the horizon. <br>- **UV visors**, the same command with the UV products: nothing at 1–250 ticks or 20–200. Baguette Fromage's half-day turn-to-turn lead (about 5,000 ticks, on turning points rather than one-tick changes) is not tested here | Microchips: `open`, a real lead in the data, untested as a trade; turn-to-turn: `dropped here` (their −7.0k live, nothing at short lags) | On the day, per family of the download: `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.lead_lag --data D --days ... --format qminers --products <family> --lags 250`. A listed lead goes into the follower's fair as the slope times the leader's move k ticks ago over the holding time, graded on held-out days against plain `wall_mid`; worth it only where the predicted move beats the half-spread |
| **A structure scan of any download** (an audit's research pass, 2026-10-26, ideas 2–3: what kind of process made each product's prices, read before any strategy) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.structure` (about 50 s): P4 round 5, P3 round 1, P4 round 1 and P4 rounds 3–4, each product's wall mid in ticks. <br>- **Repeats** (every 100-tick run of moves hashed and looked up in every other product-day): round 4's days 1 and 2 are round 3's days 1 and 2, 100% of the windows of the 10 products that move, at offset 0; nothing else repeats in any dataset. <br>- **Grids and exact jumps:** OXYGEN_SHAKE_EVENING_BREATH and ROBOT_IRONING sit on multiples of 10 on every tick of all three days (moves of exactly 10 are 80–82% of their moves), OXYGEN_SHAKE_CHOCOLATE on day 2 only; ROBOT_DISHES on multiples of 100 on 88% of day 4, where 99% of its 750 large moves are exactly 100 (the jumps of "Fading jumps that snap back"). <br>- **Moves:** P4 round 5's other products are random walks (lag-1 autocorrelation −0.02 to +0.01, variance ratio at 1,000 ticks 0.56–1.19); Resin, Pepper Root and Osmium revert hard (lag 1 −0.42 to −0.51, VR at 100 0.01–0.08), Kelp and ROBOT_DISHES half as much (−0.27, −0.23). The one periodogram peak far above the shuffled returns' (5–7): Pepper Root at 10.0 ticks, 70.5 times its neighbours — its drift of 0.1 a tick (1,000 a day) rounded to whole ticks, not a cycle. <br>- **Trade arrivals** are Poisson-like everywhere (gap CV within 0.1 of √(1 − p) but on VEV_5200's 11 trades a day, dispersion per 100 ticks 0.81–1.14), but on P4 round 5, 40 of the 50 products trade on exactly the same ticks every day (244 a day), and the Microchips and the Pebbles on two other shared sets (190 and 215): one trade clock per group, not per product (VEV_4500 / 5000 / 5100 share one too). <br>- **Day starts:** every Prosperity day continues the last (gaps of 0–3 ticks on P3, up to 45 on P4 round 5 against a day's travel of 500–3,000); P4 round 5 opens every product at 10,000. <br>- **On other data:** the mock round's public days (`python/experiments/mock_round/private/generate.sh`, then `uv run python python/experiments/mock_round/private/disguise.py`, then `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.structure --plugin python/experiments/mock_round/results/repo_qminers_format.py --format repo_qminers_format --data python/experiments/mock_round/public/data --days all`): CALDERA's large moves are exactly 60 ticks = 6.00 on 74% (16% of all its moves), its wall mid on multiples of 2.00 on 86% of ticks, and it opens every day at 1,300 (the simulator restarts) with gaps of −600 and +780 from the close. ABIDES (`uv run python -m experiments.abides_days generate --days 10`, then `--data data/abides --days 1`): the bounce, then a trend (VR 0.73 / 1.03 / 2.49 at 10 / 100 / 1,000), trades clustered (dispersion 3.00), every day opens near 100,000 with gaps of −261 to +474 (a reset; re-run 2026-10-28 on the regenerated days, R13). UChicago's `Case2.csv` (six price columns, no book) needs a loader of its own and was skipped; so was the second mock round's public data (`l2.tsv`, no plug-in) | `measured`: the readings that say which kind of process each product is, before any strategy | On the Arena's download, in the first hour and on each round's new days: `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.structure --data D --days ... --format qminers` (or `--plugin` for a plug-in not yet registered). A repeat of an earlier day: the round is replayed data, grid on the old day too. A grid or exact jump size: `jump_min` and the take thresholds in those units. A periodogram peak: check it isn't a drift rounded to the tick |
| **Fixed sums found by search, with held-out days** (Alpha Search's integer-weight search in [−3, 3] within a family, `prosperity4-alpha-search/README.md:124-141`; the wiki's `concepts/baskets-and-fixed-sums.md` asked for it) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.structure` (its `sums` section): every canonical weight vector within each name prefix (8,156 for five products), scored by tightness w'Sw / Σ wᵢ²Sᵢᵢ of the wall-mid levels on the first day, then the sum's mean and sd on the other days; an identity needs tightness below 0.05 every day and a level within 2 sds. <br>- **P4 round 5:** Pebbles `XS + S + M + L + XL` = 50,000.0, sd 0.5, tightness 0.000 on all three days: an identity. SNACKPACK_CHOCOLATE + VANILLA: tightness 0.043 / 0.020 / 0.039 but means 20,025 / 19,927 / 19,870 (sds 32–48): a mirror within each day whose level moves. Every other family's best vector is tight on the search day only (Oxygen 0.053, then 0.50 and 1.56; UV 0.066, then 0.73 and 0.90) or not at all (0.13–0.28); among all 1,225 pairs only chocolate + vanilla holds on the held-out days. Alpha Search's UV basket `3 MAGENTA + 2 AMBER + RED` (`--weights`, `README.md:146`): tightness 0.203 / 0.435 / 0.948, sd 641–985 against the legs' 415: no identity. <br>- **The package take on the Pebbles identity** (DU's rule, `prosperity4-du-trading/round5.py:952-1039`: sell all five at the bids above the level, buy at the asks below): the bids never sum above 50,000; the asks sum below it on 160 / 135 / 152 ticks a day by 2.4–2.6, a gross upper bound of 1,856–2,164 a day (the excess times the size the touch and the limit of 10 allow). <br>- **P4 rounds 3–4:** VELVETFRUIT_EXTRACT − VEV_4000 = 4,000.0 and VEV_4000 − VEV_4500 = 500.0 exactly on all six days (the deep in-the-money vouchers are the underlying less the strike); VEV_4500 − VEV_5000 tight within each day (0.001–0.005) around a level that moves from 493 to 498 (its time value). The package take on them: 0 ticks. <br>- **Controls:** P3 round 1's best vector 0.794 / 0.880 / 1.101, P4 round 1's 0.99, the mock round's 0.93: nothing | `measured`: the search finds the one exact identity and the mirror from one day and rejects the rest on held-out days. The identity is worth its implied fair for each member (`basket` mode with legs of weight −1), not the package take | On the Arena's download with several related products: the same command's `sums` section (`--group A,B,C` for a family the prefix doesn't name, `--weights` for a published basket). An identity goes to `python/experiments/prosperity/baskets.py --basket` on held-out days; a mirror whose level moves takes its level from the day so far |
| **Generator fingerprints: inner-order offsets, shared noise streams, hidden depth** (an audit's research pass, 2026-10-26, idea 3; Leo-Hawking's two-layer fair, `references/online-snapshots/Leo-Hawking_IMC-Prosperity-4-Review.README.md:232-244`) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.structure` (its `fingerprints` section). <br>- **Inner orders sit on a lattice around the wall mid:** Osmium's at −7.5 / +8.5 / +7.5 / −8.5 ticks on 20 / 20 / 19 / 19% of its 38,982 inner levels (P4 round 1, three days), Pepper Root's at ±6.5 and ±6 (12% each), Kelp's at +0.5 / −1 / 0 / +1 (29 / 28 / 19 / 17%), Resin's at ±4 (35% each) and ±2 (12%); Hydrogel ±7.5 and ±8.5 (93% on the four). <br>- **Products share noise streams** (identical inner (side, volume) multisets on the same tick, against the second product a tick later): Kelp and Squid Ink 44% against 2%; Osmium and Pepper Root nothing. P4 round 5's 50 products draw from five streams, which follow their inner volumes' ranges: Galaxy Sounds, Oxygen Shakes, UV visors and PANEL_1X2 (16 products, volumes 2–25) identical on 100% of ticks; the other panels, Sleep Pods and Translators (14, 2–15) on 77–100%; Microchips and Robots (10, 2–8) on 40–90%; Pebbles (3–15) on 92–100%; Snackpacks (2–40) on 100%; a tick apart 4–17%. The audit's "across groups 0%" was Pebbles against Snackpacks, two streams. Vouchers VEV_4500 / 5000 / 5100 share one on 18–39%. <br>- **Hidden depth** (trades at a price with no displayed level; trades larger than the volume shown there): P3 round 1 3.4–3.8% (1.7–2.1% inside the spread) and 2.0–6.1%; P4 0–1.1% and, larger than shown, 0–2% where a product has hundreds of trades, but 7.7% on VEV_5300 (285 trades) and 23.1% on VEV_5200 (65) in rounds 3–4 (`--data p4 --days 3 4`; the fourth audit's R6); ABIDES 1.5% and 2.8%; the mock round 0–0.5% and 0–1.1% | `measured`: Prosperity's inner orders are a generator's noise, drawn per stream, which is why the wall mid beat sized and weighted mids; a product's family is the stream, not the name | On the download: a product whose inner orders sit at fixed offsets is a generator's book (`wall_mid` and the offsets give its fair; Leo-Hawking's correction is untested); products sharing a stream share a generator and maybe a regime; hidden depth well above Prosperity's 0–6% on a product with hundreds of trades (a thin one reads up to 23% on Prosperity's own vouchers) means orders the book doesn't show, so size takes from probes, not the book |
| **Takers that recur across days** (Baguette Fromage, P4 round 2: takers "reappeared at identical timestamps, with identical side, and with identical size, on consecutive days", 97.7% of Osmium's of size 7 and up, none in round 1 (claims), `references/prosperity4-baguette-fromage/README.md:557-668`; an audit's research pass, 2026-10-26, idea 1) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.repeats` (about 15 s): per product, the share of day d's (timestamp, side, size) market trades that day d + 1 has too, the side read from the same tick's book (at or above the ask a buy), against day d + 1 shifted by 20 random offsets. <br>- **P4 round 2:** Osmium's trades of size 7 and up recur on 103/105 = 98.1% and 105/108 = 97.2% (all sizes 72.5% and 42.9%), against a baseline of 0.1–0.3%; Pepper Root's on 57.5% and 76.3% (all sizes 53.8% and 73.8%). Two days on: 44.7% of Osmium's day −1 trades are on day 1, and 13.5% of those that skipped day 0 (Pepper Root 38.7% and 15.7%), so the chain is mostly but not only day to day. The audit's 91–93% read a trade's side only when both sides of the book were shown; reading the hit side alone gives Baguette Fromage's figure. <br>- **Everywhere else, chance:** P4 round 1 0–1.2% (baseline 0.1–0.4%), P4 rounds 3–5 0–1.1%, P3 rounds 1–2 and P2 round 1 within 0.5 points of their baselines (Kelp 5.8–6.2% against 5.6–6.2%: small sizes at busy ticks). <br>- **Where they print:** Osmium's recurring big trades pay 8.1 past the wall mid (median 8.5, the wall itself with a spread of 16), against 31.7 lots of visible depth, never on an empty side; the 5 that don't recur print the same way. Pepper Root's 2.5 past (median 5.8). <br>- **What it is worth:** in a replay, nothing beyond a quote at the touch: the taker meets the recorded book, so a quote inside the wall already fills against it under `--match-trades all` and `worse`, and the prediction changes no fill. On a platform whose takers lift a far quote on an emptied side (probe 14), Baguette Fromage cleared the visible side just before the predicted taker and posted an extreme quote when `p_repeat × q × edge > c_clear` (the clearing cost); they estimate 70,000–100,000 more in round 2 in backtest (claim). The scan prints the next day's predicted events (202 for Osmium after day 1, 105 of size 7 and up; `--csv` writes them all) | `measured`: a generator's replayed taker schedule in one P4 round, absent in every other Prosperity round; `dropped here` as a trade, since a replay can't reward it | On the day, with two or more days: `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.repeats --data D --days ... --format qminers`. A share far above the baseline says the takers follow a schedule that repeats (and that the scored day may repeat it too: probe 15). Worth building only if probe 14 shows takers lifting far quotes: then the predicted events into the bot as a table, and the clear-and-post rule gated on `p_repeat × q × edge > c_clear` |

Arrivals are memoryless at the tick scale, so flow features carry nothing here. In the paper memory pays (45–90% over a memoryless network even with a plain exponential kernel), but its simulated flow clusters by construction, with branching ratios 0.75–0.90 | `dropped here`: nothing to build while the data has no clustering | Run the same script on the Arena's days once their loader exists: a ratio well above 1 on a product (bursts) makes a fast and a slow decaying trade count worth trying as a `make_edge` adjustment, as a `--set` grid |
| **Orders that arrive a tick late** (the second audit's A6: every backtest mode matches an order against the snapshot the bot saw; an exchange may apply it a step later) | 2026-10-26, `uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 1 --data p3 --delay N --match-trades all\|worse` (`--engine rust` prints the same): the template on P3 round 1 makes 72,674 / 63,302 (`all` / `worse`) on time, 40,472 / 31,218 one tick late and 36,336 / 28,014 two ticks late. Per day at one tick, Resin falls from 17–18k to 8.5–8.9k (its takes against the fixed fair arrive after the price has moved back), Kelp from 5.7–6.7k to 4.3–5.5k. Part of the loss is not the late prices but the limit: a late order is checked again on arrival, against a position its own earlier late orders have moved, and a failure rejects every order for its product (a race). The template quotes all the room under its limit, so one tick late it races on 3,248 of day 0's 10,000 ticks (the second audit's follow-up, 2026-10-26). **Capping the quotes** (`--delay 1 --grid PARAMS.KELP.make_size=None,40,30,20,15,10`, the same for Resin, `--engine rust`): 15–20 is best for both on every measure and on each held-out day, Kelp +2,228, Resin +2,946; both at 20 make 45,534 / 34,282 against 40,472 / 31,218, with 1,001 race ticks on day 0, and 72,670 / 63,306 on time against 72,674 / 63,302. A delay scenario in a grid (`--scenario late:delay=1`) keeps Kelp's `make_edge` order (1.5 > 1 > 0.5 on day 0) <br>- **Counting the orders on their way** (2026-10-31, the template's `DELAY`, `--set DELAY=1 [--set ORDERS_REST=True]`, `--delay 1`, `--engine rust`, the three days summed): **resting**, 40,732 → **42,916** under `all` and 34,375 → **35,792** under `worse`, the race ticks 22,475 → 1,752 (`all`; with the engine's breach check fixed the same day, an order cancelled but filled before its cancel landed makes a race, not a breach: 37,476 with 13,033 breach ticks before that fix); the late orders no longer collide with the bot's own re-posts. **Per tick** a quote is gone the step after it arrives, so only the takes on their way are counted: 40,472 → 41,038 (`all`), 31,218 → 31,477 (`worse`), race ticks 9,745 → 9,124; counting the quotes too, at their full size in case they all fill, left the bot quoting every other step (24,588, dropped here). The races left are resting orders the bot cancelled that fill before the cancel lands. | `open`: nothing to change while the Arena's timing is unknown; the cost is about half the PnL, most of it Resin's late takes and the races. A `make_size` of 20 costs nothing on time alone, but against a resting copy of the template (`--scenario war:resting,vs=python/bots/template.py`) it loses 11,680 (26,904 → 15,224): keep the full room unless orders arrive late (`TODO.md`, "Decisions") | Probe 3: if their exchange applies orders late, backtest and grid with `--delay`, re-grid `take_edge` (a take that arrives late needs a wider edge) and `make_size` (probe 4 says whether a breach rejects the product or only the order) |

The template as committed trades 53,770 lots in three days, so a fee of 0.5 a lot takes it from 72,674 to 45,788 and 1 a lot to 18,904 (Kelp trades near 2,000, so 1 a lot is about 5 bps). Kelp's best `make_edge` is 1.5 under each (84,512 / 60,460 / 36,408 against the committed 0.5's 72,674 / 45,788 / 18,904): the fee widens the gap to the committed setting, from +16% to +32% and +93%. Resin's `make_edge` 2 stays best until 1 a lot, where 4 overtakes it (20,354 against 18,904); its `take_edge` 1–2 stays best under each <br>- **Maker and taker rates** (2026-10-27, `--maker-fee`/`--maker-fee-bps`, negative a rebate; `uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 1 --data p3 --grid PARAMS.KELP.make_edge=0.5,1,2 --grid PARAMS.KELP.take_edge=1,2,4 --modes all,worse --scenario both1bp:bps=1 --scenario rtg:bps=2,mbps=-1 --scenario rtgworse:match=worse,bps=2,mbps=-1 --engine rust`): Ready Trader Go's rates (takers 2 bps, makers a 1 bp rebate) leave the template as committed at 54,138 under `all` against 42,468 with 1 bp both ways and 72,674 with none; 41,165 under `worse`. A maker-heavy bot gains from the split, and it changes no choice: `make_edge` 1 still leads under `all` (62,186) and 0.5 under `worse` (41,165), and a wider `take_edge` (2, 4) never helps. <br>2026-11-06: a fee per order placed in both engines (`--order-fee PER_ORDER`, the scenario's `ofee=`, a plug-in's `ENGINE` `order_fee`; charged for each new order the exchange accepts, filled or not, after the caps and the limit check; parity recorded), and calibrate's refusal now points at it (IDEAS.md, "Top-ups where every order costs a fee"). | `open`: no fee is known. A fee of a few tenths of a spread a lot halves a market maker's PnL and moves the best edges wider; the takes hold up | "Are there fees…" in `ON_THE_DAY.md`: if the Technical Rules charge one, backtest with `--fee` / `--fee-bps` and re-grid `make_edge` first, then `take_edge` |

The first version's note (a ratio of 1.5 on the pooled days) fired on 12 product-rounds, all noise (Resin's nearly fixed wall mid, round 0's short days, a voucher with 2 trades per 1,000 ticks); requiring every day to agree, leaving out a fixed wall mid's volatility and fewer than 300 trades, it fires on none | `dropped here` as a strategy: Prosperity's generator has no time of day. Replay is `arena.engine` on the same seed's recording without the bot. <br>- **Kelp, default mode:** replay `all` 37.1k, `worse` 12.1k, `none` 0; in the market 13.5k. **Replay `all` overstates by 2.8 times, and `worse` is within 10%**: under `all` our quote takes a share of every trade at its price, even when the trade went to an older order ahead of it. The template's fills from noise takers earn +1.0 a unit against the fundamental in both; replay just gives it 1.9 times the volume. Its takes of the limit traders' orders earn about 0 against the fundamental (the wall mid's rounding makes them look like 1 tick of edge). <br>- **Resin:** replay `all` 105.2k, `worse` 101.7k; in the market 115.1k (+9%): there, our quote inside the walls is the touch, and the takers who would have hit a worse price trade with us. <br>- **`--resting`:** Kelp 36.3k in the market against 25.7k replayed under `all`, Resin 116.3k against 102.5k. The maker cancels and re-posts every tick, so a resting quote at its price gets ahead of it. That is the optimistic queue the `docs/TODO_DONE.md` item describes, measured: the engine's back-of-queue estimate understates fills by about 40% on Kelp. <br>- **A maker that skews 0.05 a lot:** Kelp 14.1k in the market (`worse` replay 10.4k); Resin unchanged. **Stale walls** (the maker re-quotes among the takers): Kelp 22.8k, against 13.5k. **Against a copy:** Kelp 6.7k (`worse` replay 6.1k), Resin 52.7k (`all` replay 53.8k). <br>- **Calibration** (`python/tests/test_sim.py`): spread, mid AC1, signature and jump share within P3 round 1's range on all three presets. Not matched: the takers' sign persistence (`C(1)` 0 against 0.1), P3's 35% buys on Kelp and Squid Ink, and the takers' cost against the wall mid (Kelp 1.3 against 0.9, Resin 2.4 against 1.5) <br>- **2026-10-18, `KELP_FLOW`**, Kelp with P3's taker flow (`NoiseTakers` buy share 0.35, buys of 2–6 and sells of 1–5 lots, persistence 0.02; P3 round 1 Kelp: buy share 0.349 by count and 0.423 by volume, `C(1)` 0.127; the preset: 0.354, 0.409, 0.127). `KELP` itself is unchanged, so the results above reproduce. Template in the market over 5 seeds (`uv run python -m arena.sim run python/bots/template.py --products KELP,KELP_FLOW --days 5 --set "PARAMS.KELP_FLOW={'limit': 50, 'fair': 'sized_mid', 'make_edge': 0.5, 'stop_loss': 1200}"`): KELP_FLOW 23.1k against KELP's 21.2k (replay `worse` 20.8k against 19.2k): the one-sided flow costs nothing and adds volume. Unsmoothed `wall_mid` replays −6,038 / −14,211 (`all` / `worse`) against +40,781 in the market; at `smoothing` 0.95 it replays 196,385 / 146,598 against 168,869, so `worse` is again the nearer (13% low) and `all` 16% high. | `measured`: in a market that reacts like this one, rank by replay `worse` in the default mode; with resting orders, the back-of-queue model is pessimistic | If probe 17 shows a reactive market: fit the presets to the download (`--spec`, then `arena.diagnose` on generated against real days), rerun `arena.sim run` with the bot, and use whichever replay mode it agrees with. If the Arena replays a recording, this row doesn't apply |

| **The kickoff prompts on a disguised round** (the second audit's A2: an agent-only mock round; `KICKOFF_PROMPT.md`'s repo prompt in an isolated copy of the repo, and its prompt without the repository in an empty folder) | 2026-10-26, `python/experiments/mock_round/` (README: rebuild and score in seconds): three `arena.sim` products disguised as CALDERA (jumps of 6.00 that snap back 84% of the time, limit 15), LUMEN (returns to a level, limit 120) and OBERON (a market maker that doesn't know the price, tick 0.02, limit 60), with a function API and orders that rest; 3 public days, 2 hidden ones scored by `private/evaluate.py` (our engine, `--resting`, `worse`). <br>- **The repo run** validated a bot 7 minutes in and finished in 18: 65,351 on the public days, **42,446 on the hidden ones** (about 21k a session on both). It read every product right from `arena.diagnose` (the jump mode on CALDERA, the level on LUMEN), and its local figure equals the evaluator's to the cent. It never tried smoothing OBERON's fair: with `smoothing` 0.9–0.95 and edges of 1, chosen on the public days, OBERON adds about 730 there and 510 on the hidden days. <br>- **The clean-room run** wrote its own backtester (18 hand-made checks) and bot in 23 minutes: 66,718 public, **42,944 hidden**, its backtester within 0.01% of the evaluator. The +498 over the repo run is LUMEN (a fair leaning 60% to the level, with takes) and OBERON (an EMA of the mid, +232 against −1). <br>- Both put about 89% of the PnL on CALDERA's jump fade, and both found it in minutes. The clean run also found that whether taken volume stays gone moves its score by a quarter (66.7k against 49.7k with it gone) <br>- **Whether what we take stays gone** (2026-10-27, `EngineConfig.deplete`, both engines): `uv run python python/experiments/mock_round/private/evaluate.py python/experiments/mock_round/results/repo_template.py --days hidden [--deplete]` (and `clean_room.py`): on the hidden days the repo prompt's bot makes 42,446 as the round ran (the recorded book restored each step) and 31,172 with `--deplete` (−27%), the clean-room bot 42,944 and 31,685 (−26%): the dry run's quarter, now from the engine itself. <br>- **Before and after the restructure** (2026-10-28, `python/experiments/mock_round/round2/`: the Halden Exchange, four products in gzipped JSON lines with a `class Strategy` API that fits no skeleton; the round-1 kickoff prompt in `claude -p`, the same tool allowlist, stopped at the first validated bot; one run each, so the gaps are within one run's noise): **on the repo at 1184150** a validated bot at T+5:12, 14 minutes in all, 57 responses and 7.38M tokens (227k without cache reads), **9,777 on the hidden days** (12,100 public, the local figure to the cent); **on the restructured tree** (ON_THE_DAY read whole in one call with `allow_large`, about 32k tokens, the prepared `qminers` bot and plug-in) T+7:14, 10 minutes, 50 responses and 6.45M tokens (223k), **8,972 hidden** (11,101 public). Against the round's market maker's 8,632 and its reference bot's 12,195. So the restructure did not make a fresh session faster or cheaper: reading cost was never the bottleneck, the rules' mapping onto the engine was (both runs spent most of their thought there). Both named the same gaps: the engine settings a format's rules fix (fees, queue, mark) can't live in the plug-in, `premise` takes none of them, the engine has no cap on all actions or on resting orders, its limit rule differs from a per-order one, and the trades' tick convention was found by reading `run_market` (the checklist now states it; the rest is in `TODO.md`, "From the 2026-10-28 rehearsal"). The after-run also found the restructure's own faults, fixed before the commit: ON_THE_DAY's size test left no room for the day's answers, the kickoff asked for `premise` before the bot existed, and "Formats are plug-ins" was cited as a section though it is a paragraph. **Why the after-run scored 805 less on the hidden days:** VANTA, 775 of it (−491 against +284), from one ADAPTER choice and not from the restructure: where the template's top-ups would pass the 4-resting cap, the after-run's ADAPTER pulls every order of the product and posts afresh, losing its queue place on a large-tick book, while the before-run's drops the posts that don't fit. With only that changed (`python/experiments/mock_round/round2/results/after/template_drop_excess.py`) it makes 9,708 hidden, 69 short of the before-run's 9,777, and 12,062 public. <br>- **The engine fit, before and after** (2026-10-30, `python/experiments/mock_round/round3/`: the Kestrel Bourse, rules that differ from the engine on purpose, four of them unstated: `worse` matching, the last-trade mark, orders a step late, the queue; a portal giving the Arena's metrics and a results ZIP; stopped once a validated bot was uploaded and the engine fitted to it): **before** (24a536a: no `ENGINE`, caps or calibrate) uploaded at T+8 and had every rule right by T+11, by reading the ZIP's timestamps and refusals and matching fills, volume, penalty and score by hand, the latency held in its own wrapper; about 15 minutes (12:07 to 12:22 by its log), 95 responses, 15.2M tokens (307k without cache reads). **After** (d586efd) uploaded at T+36, and `experiments.prosperity.calibrate --vary delay=0,1` fitted all twelve portal figures at 0.0% within a minute (circular, the audit's round 8 found: the mock portal scores with our own engine; against the round's `mm_bot`, whose cancels its wrapper and the portal handle differently, the best fit was 30% off and wrong on delay, and calibrate still printed an ENGINE. Since 2026-11-03 it refuses above 5% on the worst figure and names the cause, and tries delay 0 and 1 by default) (`worse`, `last`, delay 1; the queue can't be told apart under `worse`); 41 minutes, 53 responses, 7.3M tokens (246k): the half hour went on writing the ADAPTER for a function API, one response cut off. So calibrate turned the fit from a few minutes of insight into one command and halved the tokens counted with cache reads (15.2M to 7.3M; without them 307k to 246k, a fifth less), but the run's length was set by the ADAPTER. **Both uploads lost** (public −4,850 and −283 credits; hidden −3,553 and −489, against the round's plain market maker's +325): with orders a step late, neither ADAPTER counted the orders in flight, so each re-posted every step, hit the 3-order cap and overfilled its limits (`TODO.md`, "From the 2026-10-30 rehearsal"). **With the orders on their way counted** (2026-10-31, the template's `DELAY`; `python/experiments/mock_round/round3/results/in_flight/template.py`, the after-run's bot with only that changed, scored as the README says): public −283 → **+142**, hidden −489 → **−389**, the breach penalty 25 → 0 (`private/check_configs.py BOT hidden`: TALC 352 → 425, OPHIR −31 → −33, BRAM −811 → −782). What is left is BRAM: under the round's `worse` matching a one-tick book's quote at the touch fills only when the book moves through it, so quoting it at all loses (the round's market maker gets no BRAM fills); a PARAMS choice, not the ADAPTER's. A stricter variant that also held the room of the resting orders cancelled in the same call (an order may fill before its cancel lands) made −299 hidden, but only by quoting less of BRAM: TALC fell to 160, and on P3 round 1 day 0 (`--resting --delay 1 --max-resting 3`) it made 10,661 against 13,340, so it was dropped here. <br>- **The fourth, the Varde Exchange** (2026-11-03, at `bc8569c`, `python/experiments/mock_round/round4/README.md`; the audit's round 8 asked for a portal that isn't `arena.engine`): its portal matches and scores in its own code, with three rules outside calibrate's grid (half of each print for resting orders, a 100-print VWAP mark, a fee per order placed, stated). A JSON-lines layout, a class API with exchange ids, cents on a 5-cent tick. The round-1 kickoff prompt, `claude -p`, alone: a validated bot uploaded at T+0:07 and the whole run 14 minutes (reading only the sections RUNBOOK names). **calibrate refused** (no setting within 5%; the best 98% off on the score) and the session pasted nothing, widened `--modes` and rebuilt the score from the ZIP by hand: the order fee was 28% of the gross, and our engine filled over twice the portal's volume under every setting. Re-run afterwards with `--vary fill_ratio=0.5,1`, the fills come within 2.6% and the volume within 10.5% (34% at best without it), the half-print rule's nearest stand-in; calibrate now prints the setting closest on the fills and suggests `fill_ratio` where ours trade more under most settings. Its bot scored €208.77 on the hidden sessions, the organisers' example €80.10. What it found, filed in `TODO.md`, "From the 2026-11-03 rehearsal": no per-order fee in the engine; calibrate's `--help` crashed on a `%` (fixed); `markPrice` read as the traded products' mean only (now every product's too); the ZIP has no time scale for `fill_curve`; the qminers ADAPTER's limit comment capped a larger limit at DEFAULTS' 50 (fixed); EXECUTION's top-ups multiply orders where each costs a fee | `kept`: run both on the day, the clean-room one as a check: neither the repo nor the tools were what limited the score, the reading of each product was. What tripped them is fixed (TODO_DONE, "A2") | A human mock round, if there's time; on the day, compare the two runs' bots on the public days before choosing what to upload |

| **A standings calculator for round 4: risk and a relative hedge sized for rank** (Qminers' 2024 winner did it by hand and halved risk, `references/wiki/concepts/risk-and-protecting-a-lead.md`; Frankfurt Hedgehogs' relative hedge, `references/prosperity3-frankfurt-hedgehogs/README.md:616`) | 2026-10-27, a synthetic field only (no Arena leaderboard yet): 30 teams, 3 rounds with medians and sds of 5k, 10k and 20k, skill persistent at ρ 0.7, a field's bet half a team's variance with loadings 0, 1 or 2, us 3 sds above the field and off the bet (`uv run python -m experiments.day.standings --synthetic --seed 4 --lead 3`: 1st by 43.6k summed, 12.3k in PnL at 10/20/30/40). The calculator reads persistence 0.52, the bet 35% of a team's variance, our loading −0.10 (true 0). **Whether less risk keeps the lead depends on our round's Sharpe**, with the mean and sd scaling together: our round at mean 35k, sd 11k: the chance of losing first at 10/20/30/40 is 37% at k 0.25, 22% at 0.5, 6% at 1, 2% at 1.5 (keep all the risk); at mean 10k, sd 19k (`--ours=-15000,0,10000,20000,35000`): 52%, 47%, 40%, 36% (the teams behind expect more than we do, so we need the mean); at mean 35k, sd 49k (`--ours=-30000,10000,35000,60000,100000`): 40%, 32%, 27%, 25%, but the mean rank rises from 1.59 to 2.28 to 3.00, and on the equal normalised score k 0.5 is best (19%, mean rank 1.20). (Re-run 2026-10-28 under `5e6023d`'s default, the field's growth spread measured from the board; the row's first figures, 35/20/6/2%, 50/45/39/36% and 38/31/27/26%, are those of `--growth-sd 0`: the fourth audit's R12.) With the mean fixed and only the sd scaling (`--mean-power 0`, the test's two-team field) less risk always protects a lead and more always chases one, as it should. The relative hedge: at k 1, holding 0.5–1 of the field's bet cut the chance of losing a place by 2 points (seed 4, us 1st: 6% to 4% under PnL at 10/20/30/40, 5% to 3% under the score); 2nd and 12.8k behind (seed 0), the chance is 51 / 45 / 55 / 37% under the four scorings and the hedge moves it by at most 2 points. A small insurance with the bet free to hold (`--hedge-edge` 0). (The row's earlier seed-0 figures, 8 / 5 / 10 / 4% to 6 / 3 / 7 / 1%, come out of no variant re-run: R12.) <br>- **Checked on a real field** (2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.standings_check --rounds4 20`): the template and the 12 non-local zoo bots, each a team trading alone; rounds 1–3 P3 round 1's days, round 4 one of 20 fresh `arena.sim` days, every other one with a regime change, so 20 round 4s played. The field's spread grew from 15,211 in round 3 to 19,370 in round 4 (×1.27), which no leaderboard shows: from the leaderboards alone the calculator predicted the mean rank well (5.01 against 5.25 realised under 10/20/30/40) but the chance of losing a place far too low (13% against 40%; at half the template's limit 23% against 65%). Given the round's scale, it gets closer under PnL at 10/20/30/40: with the scale the zoo itself showed on 5 held-out simulated days (×1.31, now `--growth-from`), the mean rank 5.65 against 5.25 and losing a place 60% against 40% (half the limit: 5.84 against 5.60, 74% against 65%), a little pessimistic, with a standard error of about 11 points over 20 rounds. Under the other scorings the scale makes it worse (the fourth audit's re-run, R12): PnL summed, losing a place 8% unscaled and 46% scaled against 10% realised; the equal normalised score, 7% and 39% against 0%. So `--growth-from` is supported under the weighted PnL scoring only, and the Arena's rule is unknown; first place and the top 3 were 0% predicted and realised throughout. A random scale (`--growth-sd` 0.5) gets losing a place closer (33%) but invents upside that didn't happen (19% top 3 against 0%). And the multiplier is the weak part: halving the limit cost the template almost no mean PnL (25,410 against 25,822), where `k` 0.5 assumes half (6.81 predicted against 5.60 realised); the variant's own backtests, fed as `--ours`, predicted it. With Squid Ink traded the template takes 96k (edge 1) to 176k (edge 0.5) of it from Gemini. The clearest case yet of a result that is only the fill model. Head to head the template wins 66.7 to 20.1 (73.7 to −8.8 under `worse`). Alone `all` / `worse`: qwen3.8 baseline −337.9 / −342.3 and −161.2 / −161.2, market maker 35.4 / 5.6; gemma4 baseline 7.7 / −1.2 and **−575.6** / −557.5, market maker 18.4 / 17.1 and **−1,979.5** / −1,950.6; gpt-oss:20b baseline −364.6 / −364.6 and 0 (it never trades), market maker **−919.5** / −888.2 and 23.5 / 9.5. <br>- **Every file passed `arena.validate`, with no repair turn, and none comes near the template.** The best local bot (qwen3.8's market maker, 35.4) makes half the template's 72.7. **The failures are new kinds:** <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *Takers that pay the spread:* both qwen3.8 baselines sum momentum, imbalance and z-score terms and cross the spread on the result. gpt-oss:20b's baseline buys 1 at the ask and sells 1 at the bid every tick, and its first market maker bids at the best ask and asks at the best bid when flat. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *Flips between the full limits* (gemma4 baseline 2): an SMA crossover and a z-score, each reached by crossing the spread. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *A units bug* (gemma4 market maker 2): an inventory skew of `pos × mid × 0.001`, 10 ticks a lot on Resin, sends its quotes through the book after every fill. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- *An ordering bug* (gpt-oss:20b baseline 2): it saves this tick's mid before reading the last one, so it never trades. <br>- **The same model and prompt can give a bot that makes money or one that loses millions:** gemma4's market maker made 18.4 and −1,979.5, gpt-oss:20b's 23.5 and −919.5. One sample says little about a model. <br>- **The template beats every local bot head to head, under both modes, and the disasters pay it:** with gpt-oss:20b's market maker in its market it makes 830.5 (alone 72.7), with gemma4's second market maker 585.2, with gemma4's second baseline 273.7 (463.6 with Squid Ink quoted). Its gains are passive fills against their crossing orders (138k Resin units at +3.18 against the wall mid next to gemma4's market maker 2). It has no fair beyond the mid: it quotes its whole room at the mid ∓ 1. Under `worse` no local bot passes gemma4's 17.1. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **phi4's bots barely trade:** three of four are within 5k of zero. Its repaired baseline runs, but tracks its positions in state it never updates, and breaches limits 50,244 times. <br>&nbsp;&nbsp;- **The zoo's worst bot:** deepseek-r1's second baseline, a long-only 3-tick momentum taker that takes the whole best ask and sells the whole best bid back. It beats plain on 58 of 116 products under `all`, 53 under `worse` and 97 resting, and loses money where plain doesn't on 13 (Squid Ink in three rounds, the rock, three P3 vouchers, five P4 round 5 products, Prosperity 2's coupon). Where it falls short: P4 round 5 under `all` (119,291 against 166,496 over 50 products) and P2 round 4's coupon (−10,051 against 0); it still loses resting on most Prosperity rounds, where doing nothing beats both. Both parts are simulator or mock-portal days (VANTA is LARGE_TICK, ±400 credits over five sessions), and the study hard-codes the suggestion (`smoothing` 0.54; `--suggest` reads 0.56 today). So a trend costs the quotes little only where fills are as optimistic as `all` or the market's own; under `worse` it turns a small gain into a loss, and where the large-tick and drift notes both fire, take-only (`make_size` 0) is the candidate to grid against the suggestion. **Re-run 2026-11-04** with the study reading `--suggest` live (`smoothing` 0.56) and `--suggest` adding that note for a large-tick product that drifts: in the market, resting, +83,368 (none) / +79,958 (trend up) / +81,967 (down) / +79,184 (shift); replayed under `worse`, −54 / −1,535 / −2,576 / −1,610. | **A one-tick reversion term in the fair** (Linear Utility's Starfruit fair, the market maker's mid plus −0.229 times its last return, `references/prosperity2-linear-utility/round1/round_1_v6.py:183-191`; DTU's round 5 fair, the mid less 0.14–0.15 times its last change on two Oxygen Shakes, `references/prosperity4-dtu-quant-lab/round_5/algorithmic/trader.py:500-506`, `:594-598`) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.reversion_term` (8 s): the term as a forecast of the wall mid h ticks ahead, w + β × its last one-tick change, β fitted leaving one day out, against the plain wall mid and an EMA (the template's `smoothing`, α 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9), by RMSE. <br>- **β reproduces:** Starfruit −0.248 (theirs −0.229); Kelp −0.27, Osmium −0.42, Pepper Root −0.46, Squid Ink −0.09; DTU's Oxygen Shakes −0.08 and −0.11. <br>- **A one-tick gain that fades:** at 1 tick it beats the plain wall mid by 3.1% (Starfruit), 3.6% (Kelp), 9.3% (Osmium), 10.6% (Pepper Root), 0.0% (Squid Ink), 0.2–0.6% (the Oxygen Shakes); at 20 ticks by 0.0–1.0%, Osmium 4.2%. <br>- **An EMA does as well or better everywhere it gains:** α 0.25 gives 3.3% and 4.0% on Starfruit and Kelp, α 0.5–0.8 gives 13.6% and 14.9% on Osmium and Pepper Root at 1 tick and 6.1% on Osmium at 20. With β = −0.25 the term is 0.75 w_t + 0.25 w_{t−1}, a two-tap average: `smoothing` in another form. Kelp stays at 0.5 <br>**2026-10-26, how often they name a noise cell** (the second audit's A4; `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.snooping_null_rates --engine rust`, about a minute: 300 null grids a row of 24 cells and 300 blocks, cross-cell correlation 0.7, nominal 5%): SPA rejects in 8.7% (t(4) tails, no autocorrelation), 10.0% (AR(1) 0.3), 22.0% (AR(1) 0.8), 10.3% and 12.7% (Gaussian, AR(1) 0 and 0.3); StepM names a cell as often. Fixing Hansen's omega across resamples (ours) is the likely cause, and the ten null grids above were too few to see it. The `worse` gate removes nothing when the second mode's noise is 0.8-correlated with the first's, as it is when both replay the same trades (6.0% against 10.0% only if independent). `arena.grid --holdout 25%` (StepM on the first 75% of the blocks, the pick kept only if it also gains on the rest) brings it to 4.7% / 6.3% / 12.3% / 7.0% / 9.0%. <br>**2026-10-26, each resample studentized by its own omega** (Romano and Wolf's Algorithm 4.2 step 3: `arena.stats.bootstrap` reruns Hansen's kernel on every resample's column; `studentize="resample"`, now the default of SPA and StepM on both engines; `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.snooping_null_rates --engine rust`, about 5 minutes, which prints both ways, and `--trials 1000`, about 20): over the six null rows above, StepM names a noise cell in 6.0 / 6.0 / 7.7 / 7.7 / 8.0 / 6.0% of grids (6.0 / 5.9 / 8.3 / 6.3 / 6.6 / 5.9% over 1,000 grids a row), against 8.7 / 10.0 / 22.3 / 10.3 / 12.7 / 10.0% (8.5 / 10.7 / 20.3 / 8.4 / 10.0 / 10.7%) with the original's omega in every resample; SPA rejects in 5.3–7.7% (5.6–8.4%). The pick with `--holdout 25%`: 3.7–5.3% (3.1–4.4%), against 4.7–12.3% (4.1–12.4%). So most of the excess is gone, the AR(1) 0.8 row's above all, but the rates still sit 1–3 points over 5%. The price is power: one cell with a planted gain of 0.3 a block (about 0.2 of its sd) is named in 51% of grids rather than 62% (1,000 grids), and kept by the holdout pick in 35% rather than 47%. Real grids: `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.snooping_tests --engine rust` gives the same verdicts (Kelp: SPA^c p 0.00 at every block length, StepM the same three cells; Osmium: none; the ten null grids: SPA^c p 0.17–0.90, StepM none); `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.selection_rules --part 1 --engine rust` makes the same picks but one ("Selection rules on unseen days"). Speed: 4,096 cells of 300 blocks take about 10 s in Rust (0.2 s with the fixed omega) and about 0.8 s a cell in Python. <br>**2026-11-02, the last 1–3 points: read at 4%** (`uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.snooping_null_rates --trials 1000 --ways resample --engine rust` with `--q` and `--alpha`, about 15 minutes a run alone; the figures above were at `--alpha 0.05`, the default before this). Over the six null rows, StepM names a noise cell in 6.0 / 5.9 / 8.3 / 6.3 / 6.6 / 5.9% at 5% (as before). Longer bootstrap blocks make it worse except at AR(1) 0.8: mean blocks of 20 (`--q 0.05`) 6.6 / 6.4 / 7.3 / 7.4 / 7.2 / 6.4%, of 30 (`--q 0.0333333`) 7.7 / 7.2 / 8.1 / 8.7 / 8.9 / 7.2%. A size-corrected level does what it should: at 4% (`--alpha 0.04`) 4.5 / 4.8 / 7.0 / 5.0 / 5.2 / 4.8%, at 3.5% 3.9 / 4.0 / 6.1 / 4.5 / 4.6 / 4.0%; SPA's rejections the same within 0.3 points. Power on the planted cell: 51.1% at 5%, 47.7% at 4%, 45.6% at 3.5%; the `--holdout 25%` pick's null rates 2.2–4.0% at 4%. Romano and Wolf's block-size calibration (their Algorithm 6.1) wasn't built: the blocks aren't where the excess comes from. **Kept: SPA and StepM are read at 4% (`arena.stats.TEST_ALPHA`) in `arena.grid`, `experiments.prosperity.selection_rules` and both studies; `ALPHA` stays 5%, the error they aim at**.
