| **Learning `make_edge` online** (Fernandez-Tapia: a market maker that learns its half-spread from its own fills, so it would fit whatever fill model the exchange has; formerly untested) | 2026-10-26, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.online_edge` (3 min, Python engine). The learner is `learn_edge` in `python/bots/research/online_edge.py`, a copy of the template, off by default. **The rule:** windows of 100 ticks in pairs, one quoting at the centre `e`, the other probing `e + 0.5` or `e − 0.5` (in turn); each passive fill (at one of our last three ticks' quote prices) scores its markout 5 ticks later; after each pair `e` moves 0.5 toward the probe if the probe scored more, and stays otherwise, within 0.5–4. Six fill models: `all`, `worse`, `worse` with a 0.5 fee, resting with `queue=prob`, one tick late, and resting against a copy of the template (`war`). **The learner's own settings were chosen on P3 round 1 day −2 alone:** 36 settings (two designs, probing both sides every pair or the centre against one probe, × window 100 / 250 / 500 × markout horizon 5 / 20 / 100 × step 0.25 / 0.5; the probe of 0.5 and the bounds fixed by reasoning), by the worst regret over the models (each model's best fixed edge less the learner, mean over the three starts, Kelp plus Resin). The pick scored 2,079 against 3,075 for the best fixed edge per product (Kelp 1, Resin 0.5–1); the both-sides design's best scored 3,274. The ranking is noisy: before a one-tick fix to the window count, the same grid put window 250 / horizon 100 first and today's pick fifth. Even on day −2 the learner's worst PnL over the models is below the best fixed edge's (Kelp 530–1,055 against fixed 0.5's 1,560). **Then, untouched,** per product, PnL summed over the days, the worst over the six models / the worst regret, for the learner started at 0.5 / 1 / 2, against the fixed edge best in hindsight on the same days: <br>- **P3 round 1 days −1 and 0.** Kelp: 1,165 / 2,732, 1,233 / 2,447, 842 / 2,821; fixed 1 (the best) 1,520 / 3,334, fixed 0.5 1,114 / 7,911, fixed 2 20 / 10,461. Resin: 14,114 / 1,507, 13,990 / 1,370, 12,715 / 2,309; fixed 1.5–2 15,024 / 1,763, fixed 0.5–1 14,882 / 1,670. <br>- **P4 round 1** (3 days). Osmium: 9,852 / 40,366, 20,774 / 29,444, 23,928 / 26,290; fixed 2 34,875 / 162, fixed 1 (as committed) 16,175 / 34,043. `war` decides it: fixed 2 makes 50,218 there, 1.5 makes 10,058. Pepper Root: all within 0.1% (229,649–229,673 against fixed 2's 229,669). <br>- **5 fresh `arena.sim` days.** Kelp: 1,270 / 8,398, 1,480 / 7,886, 0 / 18,544; fixed 1 2,631 / 8,792. Resin: 51,620 / 4,095, 52,210 / 4,625, 48,895 / 12,667; fixed 0.5–1 51,533 / 2,048. <br>**Where the edge ends** (mean over days): on Kelp it finds each model's best from any start, within the first quarter of the day: `worse` 0.5–0.75 (best 0.5), `all` 1.25–1.5 (best 1.5; 1 and 1.5 make the same quote off a half-integer fair), one tick late 1.5 (best 1.5), resting 0.7–1 (best 1). On Resin and Osmium it wanders (Resin's ends spread 0.5–3.5 on the sim days). **Two failures:** (1) markouts aren't PnL in a shared market: under `war` on day −2 it ended at 1.5 from every start, where 0.5 made the most; on Osmium it settles at 1.3–1.7, where 2 makes 50,218 and 1.5 only 10,058. (2) No fills, no signal: started at 2 on the sim's Kelp under `worse`, neither window ever fills, so it stays at 2 all day and makes nothing. Telling it the fee (`learn_cost` 0.5) moved Kelp by at most ±160 | `dropped here`: its worst case over the models, the insurance it was meant to buy, is below the best fixed edge's everywhere except trivially (the sim's Resin from 0.5–1, Pepper Root). It does recover from a wrong start (Kelp from 2 on P3: 842 / 2,821 against fixed 2's 20 / 10,461), and on Kelp from 0.5 or 1 its worst regret is below every fixed edge's out of sample, but a `make_edge` gridded under the playground's mode stays the plan | Only for a product with no usable history, or as a fill-model probe in the playground: run it and read where `e` goes (Kelp: `worse` → 0.5, `all` and late → 1.5, within a quarter of a day). Kelp's quote at 1 tick takes 0.10 lots a side a tick at 1 lot, 0.32 at 10, 0.35 at 20: past 10 lots size buys little. **Squid Ink's large prints are adverse:** the lots from 20 to 50 are worth −1.0 / −2.5 / −1.1 at 0.5 / 1 / 2 ticks. <br>- **ABIDES (median trade 51 lots, limit 100):** at 0.5 ticks 9.3 lots a tick at Q = 51, 16.0 at 102; the first 51 lots are worth −0.59, the next 51 −0.62, those from 102 to 255 −1.08 and beyond −1.6 to −5 (re-run 2026-10-28, R13): the bigger the quote, the worse its marginal fills | `measured`: on Prosperity size costs nothing per lot (Squid Ink's 20+ lots aside); on ABIDES the marginal lots get steadily worse with size | Half 1 on the download: if the marginal value falls below d for some Q, cap `make_size` near the Q before it (then `experiments.prosperity.quote_size` against rivals); compare with the ZIP's own fills by size once its fill sizes are known |
| **Implementation shortfall and missed-trade cost per order** (Harris `literature_md/harris-trading-and-exchanges/harris-trading-and-exchanges.md:9853-9859`, `:10018-10022`: value each order against the fair when it was decided, and its unfilled lots against the fair's move to the next decision; an audit's idea 8, 2026-10-26) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.shortfall` (2 s; `--engine rust` the same): the template as committed on P3 round 1 with `--delay` 0, 1 and 2, summed over the three days (price units). The decision is the order's first working tick less the delay (the engine records orders on arrival). <br>- **The fills' edge at the decision reproduces the PnL a delay costs:** Kelp −4,434 against the PnL's −4,407 at delay 1 (101%) and −6,743 against −6,190 at 2 (109%); Resin −22,011 against −27,795 (79%) and −24,606 against −30,148 (82%); the rest is the positions carried to the close. <br>- **The delay costs volume, not price:** Kelp's filled lots fall from 29,542 to 20,906 and 17,441, Resin's from 24,228 to 10,057 and 9,082, while the edge per lot holds or rises (Kelp 0.64 → 0.69, Resin 2.01 → 2.64). The fair's move between decision and arrival takes only 1.2–1.7k. Much of the lost volume is in no order file: the engine rejects a product's new orders on a race (6,241 of Kelp's 30,000 ticks at delay 1, 10,275 at 2). <br>- **The missed-trade cost says nothing for a market maker:** 99% of the quotes' lots never fill (Kelp 18,003 of 2.84M), and their summed cost swings with the quotes' skew (Kelp's quotes +4.5k at delay 0, +13.2k at 1, +10.4k at 2; its takes −4.6k / −9.6k / −11.2k, the lots it didn't take would have lost as Kelp reverts). Read it per take, not per quote. <br>- With `--data D --days ... --results ZIP [--delay N]` the same table for one run, from our `--out` folder or the portal's results ZIP through `fill_curve.py`'s column map (the --out folder of P3 round 1 at delay 0 gives the in-memory numbers exactly) | `measured`: on Prosperity a late order loses fills, not price | On the first upload's results ZIP, with our `--out` of the same file and days beside it: if the ZIP's edge at decision is below ours with as many filled lots, its fills are later or worse (try `--delay`); if its filled lots are fewer, the fill model is (`IDEAS.md`, "A fill-rate curve"'s queue share). `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "How is PnL scored?" |
| **Fills arriving in several family members at once, and whether family inventories net** (Baguette Fromage README 909–911: "if we got filled, we often got filled in many assets at once"; an audit's idea 17d; it feeds the Guéant `Γ` row below) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.family_fills --engine rust` (13 s): the template on P4 round 5, every product given the data's limit (10) and DEFAULTS otherwise, per tick (`all`) and `--resting`; a family is the products sharing their name's first word (ten families of five). <br>- **The market prints in whole families at once:** every trade tick of the round has trades in exactly 5, 10, 40 or 45 products (day 2: 372 ticks of one family, 219 of the same 8 families together, 10 of 9; days 3–4 alike). Eight families share one schedule of trade ticks; Microchips and Pebbles each have their own. <br>- **So our fills cluster:** per tick, 2+ members of a family fill in the same tick 4.0–5.7 times as often as if they filled independently (Galaxy 1,000 ticks against 248 expected), every such tick all one way; the market's own prints cluster as much (4.3–5.5). With `--resting` our fills are no more clustered than chance (0.97–1.00; Snackpacks 3.3), but that mode loses 0.1–4.3M a family a day on this round (the resting quotes are crossed on a flickering book, the known loss on jumpy products), so it is not a usable bot. <br>- **The inventories build together but carry no extra risk:** per tick the members' positions correlate +0.67 to +0.99 and \|Σ position\| / Σ \|position\| is 0.80–0.99, but in 8 families the members' wall-mid changes are uncorrelated (±0.04 at 1 and 100 ticks), so the family's carry variance is the sum of its members' (ratio 0.97–1.03). **Pebbles and Snackpacks** move against each other (−0.20 and −0.19 at 1 and 100 ticks: Pebbles sum to a constant), so the same-signed positions hedge: carry variance 0.18–0.19 (Pebbles) and 0.24–0.30 (Snackpacks) of the sum | `measured`: fills cluster across a family because the market's trades do; where members' prices are independent the clustered inventories add no risk, and in the two negatively correlated families they hedge | On the download: section 0 (do trades hit several products at once?) and the netting table. A family skew (`Γ`) is worth building only where members' moves correlate positively and the fills cluster; with negative correlation it would loosen the skew, not tighten it |
| **A nameless informed-trade detector** (Frankfurt's rule before names, `references/prosperity3-frankfurt-hedgehogs/README.md:270`, `:311`: a trade of one size at a new running daily extreme, a buy at the low or a sell at the high; an audit's idea 4) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.informed` (12 s; P3 round 5, Kelp, Squid Ink and Croissants, Olivia's name as the answer key). A (product, size) class is informed on a day if its trades at extremes move at least 3 times the larger of the product's own reversion after a new extreme and its half-spread at 1,000 ticks, with 80% hits; chosen if so on both training days (leave one day out). <br>- **Size alone fails on Kelp and Squid Ink, as the audit found:** their 15-lot trades are 29–45 a day with a mean move at 1,000 ticks of −0.7 to +0.1 (Kelp, hit 33–42%) and −1.9 to +2.1 (Squid Ink, 43–58%); at an extreme the same size moves +1.0 / +7.0 (Kelp) and +39.8 / +59.8 / +15.5 (Squid Ink) on 2–3 trades a day. On Croissants size alone works: the 3-lot trades are 2 a day, all Olivia's, +17 to +23. <br>- **Held out:** Croissants' 3-lot class is chosen on every fold and flags exactly Olivia's 6 trades (precision 6/6, recall 6/6, +17 to +23). Kelp and Squid Ink are chosen only on the fold that holds out day 2 (day 2's classes miss the rule: Kelp's 15-lot move +1.0 against a bar of 4.5, Squid Ink's hits 50%), where Kelp flags 3 trades (2 of them Olivia's, +1.0) and Squid Ink 5 (1 hers, 40% hits): recall 2/7 and 1/7. <br>- **Copying the flags** (first by writing them into the trades as a name and copying it; since 2026-10-27 through the template's own `copy_sizes`, on trades with every name blanked, which gives the same numbers once it checks the extreme at the fair the trade printed against, not the next tick's: a buy at the low lifts the next fair off it, and the next-tick version made only +1.3k): +38.3k over plain market making on the product-days it chose (Croissants +55.1k, as the name gives; Kelp −10.5k, Squid Ink −6.2k on day 2). Copying Olivia by name on all 9 held-out product-days: +40.2k (Croissants +55.1k, Squid Ink +9.2k, Kelp −24.1k). <br>- **ABIDES** (`uv run python -m experiments.day.informed --data data/abides_named --days 1 --informed ValueAgent --engine rust`, after `uv run python -m experiments.abides_days generate --days 10 --names --out data/abides_named`: the same 10 days with the agents' types as buyer and seller): no class is chosen on any fold. Value agents' trades, on the side they took, move +12 to +28 cents their way at 1,000 ticks against a half-spread of 0.5, with 54–67% hits, over 7,600–8,300 trades a day (re-run 2026-10-28 on the regenerated days, R13; on the first, +11 to +37 and 53–65%): informed on average, not right on nearly every trade, and in no size class at extremes. Copying them by name loses 426–505k a day: `copy` turns the whole limit on each trade, which suits one trader with a few trades a day, not a crowd | `measured`: the detector recovers the name only where the informed trader has a size of its own (Croissants); on Kelp and Squid Ink three days are too few to choose her class held out; ABIDES's informed flow is a crowd the rule can't see | If the Arena's trades carry no names: `uv run python -m experiments.day.informed --data <folder> --days <days> --format qminers --bot <our bot>` on the download. Trade a chosen class with `copy` (it needs a filter in the template first: size and new extreme in STRATEGY) only if it is chosen on every held-out day and its move beats what market making makes there. A crowd that is right on average needs a flow signal instead (`experiments.day.predict`) |
| **A feed beyond the book as a fair term** (an audit's idea 5: for any numeric column besides the book, its level and change against the wall mid at the same tick and ahead, a leave-one-day-out fit, an event study) | 2026-10-27, `uv run python -m experiments.day.predict --part 1` (2 s): P3 round 4's observations file for MAGNIFICENT_MACARONS, read from the data folder (`arena.formats.prosperity` hands the bot an empty Observation). <br>- **The foreign bid and ask are the price:** their one-tick change correlates +0.88 with the wall mid's at the same tick on every day, and nothing ahead (+0.00 at 1–100 ticks). Fitted on two days, the wall mid moves b = 1.00 per unit of the foreign price on the third, with out-of-sample R² 0.76–0.80 tick by tick and 0.99 over 20 ticks; around their largest changes (6+) the wall mid moves +6.4 at the same tick and nothing before (−0.2 at −5). <br>- **Sugar:** +0.55 at the same tick, b = 7.7–9.3 a unit, R² 0.29–0.42 held out; nothing ahead. **Sunlight:** −0.02 at the same tick, −0.12 at 100 ticks ahead but not on every day; after its largest rises the wall mid falls 13.8 over 100 ticks (500 events). Tariffs and transport fees change a few times a day and carry nothing. <br>- **ABIDES's fundamental** (the answer key, read as a feed for an upper bound; `--data data/abides --days 1 --part 1`): +0.01 at the same tick, rising to +0.05 at 100 ticks on every day; over 100 ticks the wall mid moves b = 0.24–0.26 of the fundamental's change (R² +0.03 to +0.14); after its largest jumps (8+ cents) +0.2 at once, +1.1 by 20 ticks, +4.5 by 100 (re-run 2026-10-28 on the regenerated days, R13): the market follows the value slowly | `measured`: a Prosperity feed is a same-tick fair input (the foreign price one for one; sugar about 8 a unit), never a forecast | If the Arena's state or download has any field beyond the book: `--part 1 --data <folder> --days <days>` (Prosperity-layout files beside the prices; another format needs its loader to keep the field). Kelp's R² is 0.08 at 1 tick and 0 at 100; Squid Ink's below 0 on some days. <br>- Sitaru's rule still trades profitably on the rare ticks where the forecast beats the half-spread: Resin 2.3–2.5% of ticks, +0.8 to +1.0 a trade on every day; Osmium 0.1%, +1 to +9; Pepper Root at 100 ticks every tick (its drift, which `target` 80 already holds). The feature is now the tick before's; Prosperity's numbers move in the third decimal. On Prosperity both cost: Kelp `lin_rho3` 1 makes +1,708 `all` and −4,005 `worse`, with momentum 0.5 −2.4k to −8.3k, and `lin_rho3` 3 with momentum 0.5 −3.4k / −9.6k (`--data p3 --grid PARAMS.KELP.lin_rho3=0,1,3 --grid PARAMS.KELP.lin_momentum=0,0.5 --set PARAMS.KELP.lin_window=100`); Osmium (`--data p4`, rounds 1–2) −0.1% to −1.2%; the gate on Kelp −0.4% (2) and −3% (1). | **The template inside ABIDES's market** (the deferred test: ABIDES's agents see our orders, so takes meet impact and momentum traders react; `experiments.prosperity.abides_market`, the template run as an ABIDES agent once a second) | 2026-10-27, re-run 2026-10-28, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.abides_market --days 10 [--set PARAMS.ABM.lin_rho3=3 --set PARAMS.ABM.lin_momentum=0.5] [--set PARAMS.ABM.gate_move=2 --set PARAMS.ABM.gate_window=50] [--resting] [--see-own] [--options ...]`, seeds 1–10, against the idle run of the same seed replayed in our engine (`all` / `worse` / resting). **Corrected 2026-10-28** (the fourth audit's R14 and R15): the agent sent its new orders with the cancels, sized on its position alone, so last second's quotes could fill too and the position reached up to three times the limit (|300|) on 8 of 10 seeds, which the replay never allows; and it saw its own quotes in the book, which a replayed day never shows. Now its new orders wait for the cancels and fit the room beside the position and the live orders (the largest |position| in every run below is 100), and its book leaves out its own orders. **As committed: −230,284 in the market (orders living a second) and −127,564 keeping its quotes**, against −21,798 / −57,872 / −362,428 replayed: worse than the replay's `worse` per second, better than its resting, losing on 9 of 10 seeds (6 of 10 resting). **The linear fair: +1,274,739 in the market, positive on all 10 seeds (94,994 to 205,475), and +1,267,377 keeping its quotes**, against 1,220,893 / 1,175,092 / 1,121,620 replayed: 104% of the replay's `all` (per seed 0.66–1.95: the same seed, not the same day, since our messages shift when the agents query the oracle, and the fundamental parts from second 64). The gate alone: +652,748 against 765,448 / 581,898 / 652,707. **What the two flaws cost:** with its own quotes left in its book (`--see-own`, the limit kept), as committed −642,291 and the linear fair +1,103,771; the first run's −617,092 and +1,066,496 (and "87% of the replay") were mostly that, the template's fair following its own full-size quote. **Other RMSC04 setups** (`--options`, the same 10 seeds; in the market / replay `all`, as committed then the linear fair): **no momentum agents** (`'{"num_momentum_agents": 0}'`) −285,449 / 32,920 and **+1,304,626** / 1,347,556; **300 value agents** (`'{"num_value_agents": 300}'`) −441,021 / −59,965 and **+1,489,360** / 1,534,327; **20 momentum agents** (`'{"num_momentum_agents": 20}'`, the most the default's value agents hold) −1,346,552 / −1,282,250 and **+1,753,606** / 1,509,255; **50 momentum with 250 value agents** −1,626,108 / −1,087,382 and **+1,385,506** / 1,512,741; **RMSC03-like** (`'{"num_noise_agents": 5000, "num_momentum_agents": 25, "fund_vol": 0.001}'`, seeds 3, 5 and 6 broken and left out) −3,159,076 / −4,900,298 and **+13,386,488** / 5,010,029 (inflated by seeds 1 and 2, +2.9M and +5.6M against 0.6M and 0.4M replayed). In each of these setups the committed template loses on every seed in the market and the linear fair makes money on every one. More momentum: 50 momentum agents against the default 102 value agents is a broken market (seed 1's price ran from 100,000 to 880,000 and down to 6,000, the fundamental near 100,000, a side of the book empty a third of the seconds); so is 25 on some seeds and 35 on every one tried, so the momentum agents need value agents to match. `abides_market` flags a broken day (its idle run's mid more than 50% off the fundamental, or a side empty over 1% of the seconds; RMSC04's own days: 0.1% and 0.1%) and leaves it out of the total. So the linear fair's edge doesn't come from the momentum agents, and inside the market it holds what the replay says. The template also gets no market trades there (`{}`), so `copy` and `copy_sizes` would be off (unused by these settings). | `measured`: in an agent market with momentum the replay's ranking holds and its numbers are about right once the bot keeps its limit and doesn't read its own quotes; the linear fair's edge is real, not an artefact of the recording | On the day, if the market reacts (probe 17): believe a replay's ranking more than its numbers; the linear fair and the gate stay the candidates where the trigger fires. Where cancels aren't instant, size new orders on the position plus the orders still live, as the ABIDES agent now does |

On P3 round 1 (`--data p3 --grid 'PARAMS.KELP.gate_ber=None,0.03,0.05' --set PARAMS.KELP.ber_window=50`) it costs −0.2% (0.05) to −2.4% (0.03). | **Prosperity 2 round 4's coconut coupons with the template's options modes** (`follow`, `smile`; `docs/TODO_DONE.md`, "Prepare the other later-round mechanics": P3's and P4's options were tested, P2's never were; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, P2 round 4's three days, the 10,000 call on Coconut, 250 days to expiry at day 1's open (Linear Utility, `references/prosperity2-linear-utility/README.md:162-170`). Both scripts gain `--strike` for an option whose name carries no strike, and `p3_smile` fits one vol when there are fewer than three strikes. <br>- **The vol is flat; the price isn't Coconut at a fixed delta** (`uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.p4_vouchers 4 --data p2 --underlying COCONUT --prefix COCONUT_COUPON --strike 10000 --expiry r4d1=250,r4d2=249,r4d3=248`): median implied vol 0.193 / 0.192 / 0.194 on a 365-day year (Linear Utility's 16% on a 250-day year is the same), intraday IQR 0.005–0.006, delta 0.50–0.55; the residual against Black–Scholes at the day's vol has an sd of 11.5–14.6 against a one-tick spread. <br>- **`smile` as tested on P3 loses** (`uv run python -m experiments.day.p3_smile --data p2 --days 4 --underlying COCONUT --prefix COCONUT_COUPON --strike 10000 --expiry r4d1=250,r4d2=249,r4d3=248 --engine rust`): the vol from the other two days is 0.1924–0.1932; the residual's fast part (less its 20-tick EMA) has an sd of 2.3–2.4 against a half-spread of 0.55, but a lag-1 autocorrelation of 0.89–0.90 (P3's: −0.04), so it drifts rather than reverting. Every variant loses in thousands over the three days: take-only −208 (`take_edge` 2) to −266 (0.5), with quotes −211 to −272 (−489 to −522 resting), hedged −357 to −486; held out −207.9. Plain market making on the coupon (no option model): +3.0 `all`, −0.1 `worse`, −134 resting. <br>- **With a long offset window it pays** (`uv run python -m arena.grid python/bots/template.py 4 --data p2 --set 'PARAMS.COCONUT_COUPON={"limit": 600, "fair": "wall_mid", "strike": 10000, "smile": (0.0, 0.0, 0.193), "underlying": "COCONUT", "expiry": 249, "take_edge": 0.5, "make_size": 0, "clear_edge": 0}' --grid 'PARAMS.COCONUT_COUPON.smile_window=20,200,1000,5000,20000' --grid 'PARAMS.COCONUT_COUPON.take_edge=0.5,2,5,10' --modes all,worse --engine rust`; one vol and one expiry for all three days): `smile_window` 20 −266,554; 200: +100k to +147k with a losing day; 1,000: +304k to +346k; **5,000: +441,929 at `take_edge` 0.5 (min day +114,476)**, 20,000: +417,878 (min day +121,558); the same under `worse` and `--scenario rest:resting` (it only takes). The pick held out on each day is the same cell (+441,929); PBO 7%, StepM names 18 of 19 cells. The offset then averages half a day, so the coupon is traded against its own slow deviation from the model, as Linear Utility traded its vol's oscillation around 16%. The delta hedge in Coconut costs 36.5k / 65.4k at 5,000, as on P3 (the same grid with `--set 'PARAMS.COCONUT={"limit": 300, "fair": "wall_mid", "hedge": True, "take_edge": 1000000, "make_size": 0, "clear_edge": 1}' --grid 'PARAMS.COCONUT_COUPON.smile_window=5000,20000' --grid 'PARAMS.COCONUT_COUPON.hedge_ratio=0,0.5,1' --scenario rest:resting`). The PBO is rank stability more than independence: its CSCV blocks (about 1,667 ticks) are shorter than the 5,000-tick window (the audit's round 8, R18c). <br>2026-11-04, a reading corrected for the guidebook (`uv run python -m experiments.day.p3_smile`, part 2; `docs/guidebook/book/data/coupon_acf.csv`): the 0.89–0.90 above is the lag-1 of the fast part (the residual less its 20-tick EMA), a half-life of about 6 ticks, so it does close, slowly against its sd of 2.3–2.4 and the half-spread of 0.55; the residual's own lag-1 is 0.996–0.997, half-lives of 163 to 255 ticks. "Drifts rather than reverting" should read "closes too slowly to fade at a 20-tick window". | `kept`: `smile` with a long `smile_window`, take-only, unhedged; `PARAMS` unchanged (no P2 round 4 entries; the coupon's expiry differs on every backtest day) | On the day, for an option: part 2 of `p3_smile` first; where the fast residual's lag-1 autocorrelation is near 1, grid `smile_window` up to 20,000 before deciding the option doesn't trade |
| **Skewing on correlated products' inventory too (Guéant's `Γ`)** (`references/wiki/concepts/many-products-architecture.md`; "Fills arriving in several family members at once" above: on P4 round 5 the template's fills cluster across a family and its members' positions correlate +0.7 to +0.99; formerly untested) | 2026-11-02, `uv run python -m experiments.prosperity.family_skew 5 --data p4 --grid 'DEFAULTS.skew=0,2,5' --grid 'DEFAULTS.family_weight=0,0.5,1' --modes all,worse --holdout last` (about 5 min, the Python engine): P4 round 5, every product at the data's limit (10) and DEFAULTS otherwise, a family the products sharing their name's first word. Each product's `target` is moved against the rest of its family: `family_weight` × Σ (the others' position over their limit) × its own limit, clamped to the limit; `skew` quotes against it and `clear` crosses toward it, so at weight 1 a member skews as if it held the family's position. `all` / `worse`: <br>- **No skew:** the bot as is 456,360 / 444,766; with the family term alone (only `clear` acts on it) 422,185 / 413,590 at 0.5 and 430,590 / 422,085 at 1. <br>- **`skew` 2:** own inventory 404,963 / 255,810; with the family 234,334 / 143,878 (0.5) and 228,800 / 172,568 (1). **`skew` 5:** 225,042 / 189,798; 65,580 / 72,206 and 76,137 / 66,535. <br>- The bot as is wins on every measure and on two of three held-out days; StepM at 4% names no cell (SPA p 0.547). Own-inventory skew costs, as on P3 ("`skew` against Kelp's long position" above), and the family's adds to the cost: the members' prices move independently (or against each other in Pebbles and Snackpacks), so their clustered inventories carry no risk worth giving fills up for. Prosperity has no family of positively correlated products that the template makes markets in (baskets and options have their own modes) | `dropped here` | Only where `experiments.prosperity.family_fills`' netting table on the download shows a family's moves correlated positively and own `skew` wins a grid (an inventory cost, a worse close): then `experiments.prosperity.family_skew` on the day's data |

The ABIDES days: µ+ +0.026, orders 19% of the 3-level depth, reaching level 0's end 16% of the time: −0.01, just short (the same on the days regenerated 2026-10-28). <br>- **The detector's baseline, 2026-11-02** (R6's second half, its inputs: `arena.diagnose`'s `spoof_before` and `spoof_snap`, in the CSV; the same runs): the Wasserstein distance between the 3-level bid share just before market buys and on every tick (the ask's share for sells, weighted by their counts), and between the share before them and after. With no spoofer anywhere: 0.000–0.019 on the products the template makes markets in (ABIDES 0.008), at most 0.001 on P4 round 5's 50 products, 0.012–0.077 on the baskets, Macarons, P2's coupon and P4's vouchers, and 0.18–0.27 on P3's volcanic vouchers. The two distances agree within 0.004 on every product, since the books are redrawn every tick and the book after a trade is any tick's: there, orders come when their own side of the book is heavier, the generator's lean, which reads exactly as a spoof would. A `--vs` spoofer can't show the tell on replays: the recorded takers don't react to its orders and the recorded book doesn't hold them; on a synthetic day a lean timed on the trades reads 0.17 / 0.25 and an untimed one about 0 (`python/tests/test_diagnose.py`) | `measured`: not on the market-making products; the positive scores read an association in a replay (the generator draws book and price around one value), while a spoof pays only where other traders' quotes react to the book, which a recording can't show. The detector reads a spoofer only against the product's own baseline, never against 0 | On the day: the column per product on the download; where it is above 0 and the market is shared, run the detector half (the imbalance before trades against after) on the first resting upload's book. A product where Kronos beats `wall_rev` on every day is a lead for `experiments.day.predict`, not a fair to ship |

## Benchmark: the template against the published P4 traders

The teams' own files in our engine (2026-10-13; commands on each team's page under `references/wiki/sources/`). **These are the teams' traders on the days they were built on, so they overstate what they did live.** DTU's 3-day backtests were 135,842 (round 3), 116,090 (round 4) and 501,498 (round 5); its live single days were +3,686, +25,351 and +702,835.

**Rounds 1–2, PnL per day.** The template is level with DTU's round 2 trader and 6–9k a day behind the best:

| Trader | Round 1 | Round 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Template (Pepper Root `target` 80) | 93.9–96.8k | 96.0–96.7k |
| DTU, round 2 trader | 95.5–97.0k | 97.4–98.7k |
| DU, `round2.py` | 100.6–102.1k | 103.0–105.0k |
| Baguette Fromage, `Phase1.py` | 101.9–102.8k | 104.2–105.6k |

**Rounds 3–5, PnL over each round's 3 days, in thousands.** "Template" here is plain market making around `wall_mid` on every product: for each product, `--set 'PARAMS.<PRODUCT>={"limit": <limit>, "fair": "wall_mid", "make_edge": 1}'`. Rounds 3 and 4 share days 1 and 2, so they hold 4 distinct days, not 6.

| Round | Products | Template | DTU | Baguette Fromage | DU |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | VEV vouchers (options) | 11.6 | 47.2 | 604.3 | 366.9 |
| 3 | Hydrogel | 29.5 | 55.5 | 148.8 | 64.8 |
| 3 | Velvetfruit (the vouchers' underlying) | 14.6 | 33.2 | 98.1 | 73.2 |
| 4 | VEV vouchers | 11.3 | 40.0 | 620.2 | 382.5 |
| 4 | Hydrogel | 27.4 | 47.2 | 161.8 | 67.0 |
| 4 | Velvetfruit | 4.9 | 28.9 | 106.5 | 74.7 |
| 5 | Robots (the round-number jumps) | 11.1 | 9.2 | 508.5 | 308.6 |
| 5 | Oxygen shakes | 76.7 | 167.1 | 147.5 | 62.0 |
| 5 | Pebbles (fixed sum) | 33.7 | 54.0 | 73.6 | 56.2 |
| 5 | Microchips | 20.3 | 45.6 | 51.5 | 46.2 |
| 5 | The other six families | 316.0 | 225.6 | 310.0 | 253.4 |
| | **Total, rounds 3 / 4 / 5** | 55.7 / 43.6 / 457.8 | 135.8 / 116.1 / 501.5 | 851.2 / 888.6 / 1,091.0 | 505.0 / 524.3 / 726.5 |

What it says, as priorities for strategy work:
1. **Options** are the largest gap: about 610k against 11k on the vouchers.
2. **Fading the round-number jumps** (the Robots in round 5): 309–509k against 11k. Almost all of it is ROBOT_DISHES on day 4, the only product-day of the round in its jumping regime ("Fading jumps that snap back", above).
3. **The spot products of rounds 3–4** (Hydrogel, Velvetfruit): 100–160k each for Baguette Fromage against 5–30k.
4. **Plain market making holds its own** on the other six round 5 families: 316k against 226–310k for the teams. It leads on Galaxy Sounds and Panels and trails mainly on Sleep Pods (54k against Baguette Fromage's 75k).

**Since 2026-10-13 the template trades rounds 3–4 with its level mode and the vouchers following Velvetfruit** (PARAMS tuned on these days, so in-sample): round 3 854k (Hydrogel 115k, Velvetfruit 96k, vouchers 642k), round 4 844k (123k, 91k, 631k), level with Baguette Fromage's 851k and 889k (re-run 2026-10-13 after the review's `level_exit` fix: round 3 was 860k, round 4 832k, held out 884k). Scored on days it wasn't tuned on, 879k over the four distinct days (`experiments.prosperity.p4_level_template`, `experiments.prosperity.p4_vouchers_template`). Under `--resting` the four days make 1,153k against 1,157k (`uv run python -m arena.backtest python/bots/template.py 3 4-3 --data p4 --resting`): it trades by crossing the spread, so the fill model barely matters. Priorities 1 and 3 are covered on P4; the Robots are next.

**Since 2026-10-13 the template's `jump` mode covers priority 2** (in-sample): switched on for every round 5 product on top of plain market making, it makes 958.8k over the three days against 457.8k without it, Baguette Fromage's 1,091.0k and DU's 726.5k (`experiments.prosperity.p4_jumps`, "Fading jumps that snap back", above). What's left of the gap to Baguette Fromage is spread across the other families.

## Not yet tested

| Idea | Where it comes from | Test, and where it would go |
|---|---|---|
| Stacking quotes at several levels to hold queue places (Chung et al.: −2.13 to +2.33 a day for a simple rule) | `references/wiki/sources/order-stacking-mm.md` | Only if the Arena's orders rest with time priority (probes 1 and 11): `--resting` with the template quoting one extra level a side. The plain ladder (`make_levels`) is tested above, "Laddered quotes"; holding queue places this way needs time priority |
| Round 1 as the prepared team's largest PnL lead, if the total is weighted raw PnL (the user, 2026-11-02) | `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Round weights, unconfirmed" (10/20/30/40%, from unused site strings) and "How is PnL scored?"; the site calls round 1 the "forgiving market". If the total is Σ weight × raw PnL, a round's lead counts in its own units: in round 1 most teams are still learning the API while our tooling grids a simple market, so our PnL lead there may be the largest of the four, and even at 10% it can outweigh later rounds' leads if PnL scales are similar. If rounds are normalised (to the best team, the field's spread) or ranked, a large win counts little more than a narrow one. **If PnL carries over** (the user, 2026-11-02: Qminers' 2025 hackathon did, its bankroll compounding across the finals rounds, as in 2024, `references/wiki/sources/qminers-hackathon-2024.md`): a carried-over score is the weighted case above; carried-over capital or limits would compound, so round 1's profit would also buy capacity in every later round, and a round-1 loss would shrink it. The 2024 winner, on a compounding bankroll, staked a quarter of it at three times its Kelly fraction for the first 1,000 bets, then folded it back in. In 2025 (the user, 2026-11-02) a team that lost its default 1,000 in a round was lent 1,000 to play the next, repaid at the end: the loss is capped at the stake while a win compounds, a convex payoff, where seeking variance in round 1 is right | Once the Technical Rules give the combination: under weighted raw PnL, run round 1 for expected PnL (every product traded, full limits where the grid holds, the grid's best by `pnl` among the cells that beat the bot as is under the playground's mode, and the round's submissions spent on PnL rather than probes after the first validated upload), with risk cheap at 10%; under normalised or ranked scoring, keep the usual pick; if capital or limits carry over, push round 1 harder still, but size it so a bad round 1 can't cut later rounds' capacity by more than the expected gain buys (the 2024 winner's aggressive-early, normal-later rule); if a busted stake is lent back as in 2025, the cut can't happen, so take the most variance the grid's cells offer in round 1 (the full limit, the widest-swinging cell that still beats the bot as is). Check on round 1's leaderboard how far the field is behind (`experiments.day.leaderboard_curves --read`), and score the choice with `experiments.day.standings`' four combinations |
| The portal's whole metrics set as an engine fingerprint, not only its PnL | `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "How is PnL scored?" (2026-10-26); the site's `--metrics` fields | On the first upload, match `cash + inventory × markPrice − breachPenalty`, fills, volume, the four \|position\| quantiles and the three `pnlSe` guesses; compare the results ZIP's fills/orders with `--out`. Done when the settings that match every figure are in the round's handover note |
| The leaderboard's cross-team PnL total, clock and field | `experiments.day.leaderboard_curves --read` (2026-10-26); `ON_THE_DAY.md`, "Is the market shared…" | After round 1, read the total across teams (flat vs rising), the `elapsedS` clock (length, spacing, resets) and the field's distribution and top drawdowns; write what each changed in the next bot into the handover note |
